# 0015 — Content expansion: the POOL data kind, families as table rules, the fuzz corpus

Status: **accepted**. The owner decided the three open points of `docs/research/expansion/README.md` section 7 on 2026-10-02 ("ja" to the recommendations, collected by the main session). Builds on decisions 0006 (data, state v3, evidence), 0009 (Team C: the extended tables and their prefix test) and 0010 (the certified CLOSURE profile). The differential loop of `docs/research/expansion/differential-testing.md`, components 1 to 4, is on `main` (PRs #72, #74, #76, #79, #80, #85).

## 1. Owner decisions (2026-10-02)

1. **Data kind.** New kinds `POOL` (6) and `POOL_DEV` (7) for the growing tables. Closure and Team C are their exact, frozen prefix: the CLOSURE and TEAM_C fingerprints never change.
2. **Fuzz corpus.**
   - It lives in the repository: specs and gzip-compressed traces under `tests/reference/corpus/`.
   - It is curated: only battles that found a defect or covered a new path go in, never the whole fuzz output.
   - The cap is about 20 MB. Anything beyond it later moves to a release artifact.
3. **Machine.**
   - Fuzz runs go in chunks of at most 10 minutes under `tools/ci/machine_lock.sh`, with a 30 s yield between chunks.
   - For measurements fuzzing pauses completely: the driver checks a pause file before each chunk and waits while it exists.

## 2. POOL data

- **Tables.** `tools/datagen/gen_closure.py --pool` writes `src/data/pool_tables.{h,c}`:
  - the extended tables (closure, then Team C), unchanged, as the prefix;
  - then the new rows, in the order the steps add them.
- **Which tables the engine reads.** It reads the pool tables under every combat kind. The kind's counts bound every id at setup and in the invariants (CLOSURE: closure counts; TEAM_C: extended counts; POOL: pool counts). So a CLOSURE or TEAM_C battle reads only its prefix, as in decision 0009 section 3.2.
- **Family parameters.** New per-id columns:
  - for items: the family and its type;
  - for abilities: the family and its type, weather or terrain.
  - They are filled for every id, including the prefix, because the engine reads them under every kind.
  - The CLOSURE and TEAM_C canonical bytes do not include them, just as 0009 kept the poison immunity bit out of the closure bytes.
- **Prefix test** (the pattern of `duoforge.data.extended_tables`):
  - every closure and Team C row is field-equal in the pool tables;
  - the CLOSURE and TEAM_C canonical bytes, recomputed from the prefix, hash to their pinned table hashes;
  - the family parameters of the prefix ids equal what the engine hard-coded before (for example Mystic Water: type booster, Water; Chople Berry: resist berry, Fighting; Aerilate: "-ate", Flying; Blaze: pinch, Fire).
- **Deriving the parameters.**
  - The generator reads them from the pinned sources with one strict pattern per family. A handler that deviates from its family's pattern fails the generator; it never guesses.
  - A Node cross-check under `DUOFORGE_PS_REFERENCE_DIR` calls the pinned handlers with every type and must agree with the generated columns.
- **Legal moves and abilities per forme.** A forme has one set in the closure and Team C tables, but the real teams use table moves that are not in their forme's set (for example Milotic's Protect, Golisopod's Sucker Punch, Kingambit's Swords Dance) and abilities beyond the set's. So the pool tables get two more columns for every forme, in an array of their own:
  - **Learnable moves:** a bitset over the pool moves. A move is learnable when the forme lists it in the Champions learnsets at the pin (`data/mods/champions/learnsets.ts`). Every entry there is `"9M"` (no event, egg or level-up source), so learnable means listed. The generator parses the block strictly and requires the pool moves it finds to be exactly the pool moves that the pinned validator accepts for the species (`legal_pool.json`).
  - **Legal abilities:** a list over the pool abilities, in slot order. They are the forme's abilities in the pinned pokedex, filtered by what the validator allows in Champions (`abilities_legal` of `legal_pool.json`) and by the pool abilities. A Mega forme has its one ability. An ability that the pin tags as not released fails the generator explicitly (Lucario-Mega-Z's Aura Guard is the known case).
  - A Mega forme is reached in battle and never set up, so its learnable set is empty.
  - Both columns are part of the canonical pool bytes, as the family columns are, and not of the CLOSURE or TEAM_C bytes.
- **POOL fingerprint.** It is composed as today: data kind, roster sizes, species and move counts, target classes, and the hash of the canonical pool bytes, which include the family columns and the legal moves and abilities per forme.
  - It changes with every PR that changes the pool data.
  - A state or recipe of another pool version is rejected explicitly.
  - For training or a certification, a pool version is later frozen as its own named profile, as CLOSURE was.
- **Setup rules.**
  - POOL: the CLOSURE rules (decision 0006 section 2.1, and the register 6 / bring 4 freeze of 0010) over the pool tables, with mixed teams and any table item, and **one change, the set rule:**
    - a member's moves are 1 to 4 distinct pool moves that its forme learns;
    - its ability is one of its forme's legal abilities in the pool tables;
    - for a Mega-capable base forme these are the base forme's (the Mega forme is reached in battle);
    - the support gate still applies to every move, item and ability, so a legal choice that is not marked is `E_UNSUPPORTED` after all validation.
  - POOL_DEV: as the other DEV kinds, so also No Ability and 4 to 6 registered members; the same moves and abilities as POOL.
  - CLOSURE, CLOSURE_DEV, TEAM_C and TEAM_C_DEV keep the set rule (the moves and the ability of the forme's one set). Their behaviour and fingerprints are frozen.
  - The member invariant of a decoded state follows the same split: moves and ability by the set under the four frozen kinds, by the learnable moves and the legal abilities under the POOL kinds.
- **Support gate.** Every new id starts unmarked. A setup that needs an unmarked mechanic fails with `E_UNSUPPORTED` after all validation, as today. A step marks only what it tested.
- **Legality.** Only format-legal ids enter the pool: they must pass `TeamValidator` at the pin, which `docs/research/expansion/data/legal_pool.json` records.

## 3. Families as table rules

The families of the research note, section 7, with their legal members:

| Family | Members in the pool after the step | Prefix members |
|---|---|---|
| Type boosters (BasePower x4915/4096, priority 15) | all 18 | Mystic Water, Miracle Seed |
| Resist berries (ModifyDamage x0.5 on a super-effective hit; Chilan Berry: Normal, without that condition) | all 18 | Chople Berry |
| "-ate" (Normal to the type, BasePower x4915/4096 at priority 23) | Pixilate, Refrigerate | Aerilate |
| Pinch (ModifyAtk/SpA x1.5 at a third of the HP or less) | Overgrow, Torrent, Swarm | Blaze |
| Weather setters, surges | the prefix only | Drizzle, Drought, Grassy Surge, Psychic Surge |

- The engine's hard-coded checks (`turn.c`: the BasePower chain, ModifyAtk, ModifyDamage, the entry abilities) become reads of the family columns. They then hold for every member.
- New weather or terrain setters (Sand Stream, Snow Warning, Electric Surge) need new field mechanics. They follow later, ordered by usage. Galvanize and Misty Surge have no legal holder.

## 4. Steps

One PR per step.

1. **P1, data**, in two PRs:
   - **P1a:**
     - the POOL and POOL_DEV kinds and the pool tables: prefix, family columns, and the new rows of section 3, all unmarked;
     - the engine reads the pool tables;
     - prefix, fingerprint and setup tests.
     - No behaviour change: the closure and Team C conformance, the certified dataset and every digest stay byte-identical.
     - New public constants (the two kinds), so a minor version bump, agreed with the other sessions before the number is taken.
   - **P1b:**
     - the legal moves and abilities per forme (section 2) in the pool tables and their canonical bytes;
     - the set rule of the POOL kinds, in the setup and in the member invariant;
     - the CLOSURE and TEAM_C kinds stay as they are, which the conformance and fingerprint tests show.
2. **P2, items:**
   - type boosters and resist berries become table rules, and the new members are marked;
   - recorded reference battles for every variant: a booster of each kind of type the closure lacked, a resist berry with and without a KO, and Chilan Berry's Normal variant;
   - the interactions with Life Orb, Chople's ModifyDamage order, and a booster under Helping Hand.
   - A spec with `"data": "pool"` is converted with the pool tables into `tests/reference/conformance_pool.h` and runs under POOL alone (no DEV fallback, so the records name the data kind). The records writer, the diff runner and the replay driver carry that third kind; random play over POOL teams (`diff_random.py`) is a separate step, as it needs team files that carry the new members (step G5 did it: `DATA_KINDS` of the driver has `pool`, a team with an id beyond Team C's is pool data, and `duoforge.reference.diff_pool_smoke` plays one that carries U-turn).
3. **P3, abilities:** "-ate" and pinch become table rules, Pixilate, Refrigerate, Overgrow, Torrent and Swarm are marked, with recorded battles.
   - Only Overgrow on Rillaboom is reachable through the setup rules now (a pool forme with Pixilate, Refrigerate, Torrent or Swarm comes with the content steps), so the recorded battle is Overgrow in pinch range; Aerilate (Normal move from Mega Salamence) is already recorded. The others are covered by a unit test of the dispatch over every ability and type and by the Node cross-check of their pinned handlers against the family columns.
4. **P4:** weather setters and surges become table rules over the prefix members. There is no behaviour change, which the conformance tests show.
   - Done: `dfi_has_entry` and `dfi_entry_ability` read the weather and terrain columns through `src/combat/ability_family.h`; Intimidate is no family and stays named. The Primal exception of the pinned weather handlers (Kyogre with Blue Orb, Groudon with Red Orb) is checked by the cross-check; no Primal forme is in the pool.
5. **G2, the rows of the 17 target teams** (`docs/research/expansion/team-gaps.md`, `data/team_gaps.json` `pool_rows`): every row they need, added at once and unmarked, so that the pool table content is fixed for the rest of the track: the formes Pelipper, Arcanine-Hisui, Annihilape, Floette-Eternal and Floette-Mega, 22 moves, the items Focus Sash, Expert Belt and Floettite and the abilities Rock Head, Flower Veil and Fairy Aura, with their learnable moves and legal abilities. No new column: a move whose callback or field no column models is mapped to a named handler id in its special column (decision 0009 section 3.3), which the turn code refuses; a step that needs a column changes the tables and the POOL fingerprint and says so. A move is marked by the step that records a reference battle with it under the POOL kind: G2 marks twelve (the data-only ones, in four battles `g2_data_moves_a` to `_d`), not U-turn and not the nine handler moves. **G5 marks U-turn** (`team-gaps.md`): the switch flag of a damaging pivot is the move's own (`dfi_pivot_moves`, one value per move, valid under the POOL kinds for U-turn: `switch_flag_max` is `DFI_SWITCH_UTURN` there and `DFI_SWITCH_FLIP_TURN` under the TEAM_C kinds), so the switch event names the move that pivots (`[from] U-turn`, `[from] Flip Turn`); a self-switch move without a flag is refused at the move (`E_UNSUPPORTED`). Evidence: `g5_uturn_a` to `_e` under the POOL kind (a knock-out, Protect, an empty bench, Emergency Exit in the same action, a Rocky Helmet that faints the user); the Flip Turn battles are unchanged. **G12 marks Floette-Eternal's mechanics** (`team-gaps.md`): Flower Veil (TryBoost and SetStatus on a Grass-type ally; the line is the public ACTIVATE event of the ability with the protected Pokemon as the position and the holder in `other`, so no new event kind and no view field), Fairy Aura (BasePower x5448/4096 for every Fairy move on the field, at priority 20 of the chain; the Mega entry shows the ability) and Floettite, with `g12_floette_mega`, `g12_fairy_aura_both`, `g12_flower_veil_a` and `_b`; Moonblast and Calm Mind (the rest of the real Floette set; their rows were modelled) are marked with `g12_floette_moves`.
5a. **G8, Throat Chop and Psychic Noise** (the first mechanics on the tail; decision 0015 section 7). Both leave the handler list: each is a **secondary kind** of the generic secondary column, plus one new column. The new column is `dfi_pool_move_flags2[]` (a byte per pool move: bit 1 the pinned `sound` flag, bit 2 the `heal` flag), added as the last part of the canonical pool bytes of the whole-pool layout (section 4.2: after the legal moves and abilities of the formes), so the POOL fingerprints change (KP f82c6cfc..., KPD 65d23ec1... on the whole-pool tables) (06e5aa0a... and 244d4a81... after step G12, whose engine rows Floettite, Flower Veil and Fairy Aura change the handler columns); the generator reads exactly the secondary text of each (`secondary: {chance: 100, onHit(target) {target.addVolatile('throatchop')}}`, and Psychic Noise's `volatileStatus: 'healblock'` secondary) and the condition facts it relies on (duration 2, Heal Block's `durationCallback` 2 for Psychic Noise, silent Throat Chop start and end) and fails on any other text. Throat Chop: `-start`/`-end` are silent, so no event; the sound moves are disabled in the request for two turns (counter 2 on the hit, one counted down by the residual of that turn, so the next request has them barred and the one after has them back); a barred move that was queued is `cant|X|move: Throat Chop` with no PP spent. Heal Block (Psychic Noise, 2 turns): `-start`/`-end|move: Heal Block` are shown (new events `VOLATILE_START`/`VOLATILE_END`, the cause `HEAL_BLOCK` for `cant|X|move: Heal Block|<move>`), every heal of the holder is refused (onTryHeal: `data/moves.ts` healblock; Leftovers and Grassy Terrain's residual heal and Sitrus Berry through the same onTryEatItem, which is why the berry is not eaten, `data/items.ts` sitrusberry, `sim/pokemon.ts` eatItem) and the moves with the heal flag are disabled in the request (Bitter Blade, Leech Life in the pool; Recover stays unmarked). The counters live in the POOL tail; a switch, a faint or a replacement clears the position's five tail fields and the occupant's Soak type (`dfi_tail_clear_occupant`). The Heal Block end is a duration handler of order 20 and Throat Chop's of order 22 (before the side conditions at 26): two blocks that end in one residual come in the speed order of their holders, and at equal Speed (a self-play mirror) the reference shuffles the pair (one `SPEED_TIE` draw, `random(start, start + 2)`) and the two `-end` lines show the outcome, so the converter KEEPS that tie as an entry that states which line is first (`heal_block_end_tie`, like `side_end_tie`; the precondition is that two of the holders end in the step, checked against the step's log, and a draw that contradicts the lines is an error) and the engine draws the `SPEED_TIE` after the callbacks of the sort and orders the pair by it. The drop rule for duration ties stays for ties without two visible lines (a tie of which fewer than two end), and `drop_reason` needs the log to apply it. Three or four equal-Speed holders ending together need the longer shuffle: `E_UNSUPPORTED` in the engine, `heal-block-tie-size` in the converter. A side-condition end tie in the same step as a Heal Block tie draws in the engine's order (side first), the reference's is Heal Block (20) first: a tape mismatch, never a silent difference. `dfi_pool_move_flags2` is the general second flags byte of the pool moves: bit 1 `sound`, bit 2 `heal`, bits 4 to 128 free for the next consumers (a step that reads another pinned flag adds its bit and the generator's name for it); Heal Block refuses every heal-flag move through bit 2 alone, so a later heal move (Recover) is blocked without more code. Correction to the research note: the Sitrus Berry is not eaten under Heal Block (it was assumed eaten). Marked: Throat Chop and Psychic Noise, by `g8_throat_chop`, `g8_heal_block`, `g8_heal_block_pair` (two blocks of different Speed) and `g8_heal_block_tie_a` and `_b` (equal Speed, the two orders of the shuffle); the request domain was also checked against Showdown with the random-play domain check over the teams of the two specs under POOL. View extension (decision 0018, tier 0 is on main): G8 fills `duoforge_position_ext.volatiles` bits `HEAL_BLOCK` and `THROAT_CHOP` (both public, for both viewers; presence of the tail counters, never the counters) and sets the supported bits 16 and 6; `duoforge.state.pool_g8` compares both viewers' whole extension with the protocol's after every step of the five G8 battles (rows derived from the `-start`/`-end`/OUT lines by `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py`, section 6.1 of 0018). New public constants (owner's OK needed): `DUOFORGE_EVENT_VOLATILE_START` 39, `DUOFORGE_EVENT_VOLATILE_END` 40, `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_HEAL_BLOCK` 15, `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_HEAL_BLOCK` 1; no view field.
5b. **G11, Soak** (the second mechanic on the tail: the soak type of section 7). Soak keeps its handler id (`DFI_SPECIAL_SOAK`; no table change, so no fingerprint changes) and the turn code implements it; it is marked.
   - **The pin.** `data/moves.ts:17186-17208`: a Water status move, accuracy 100, flags protect, reflectable, mirror, allyanim, metronome; `onHit(target)`: a target whose `getTypes().join()` is `Water`, or for which `setType('Water')` returns false, gives `-fail|target` (the argument is the target, not the user; the move still animates, so no `[still]`) and `null`; otherwise `-start|target|typechange|Water` (no `[from]`). `Pokemon.setType` (`sim/pokemon.ts:2109-2129`, without `enforce`) refuses Arceus and Silvally (species numbers 493 and 773; no pool forme is one, the code still follows the rule) and a Terastallized Pokemon (no Terastallization in the format). The type lasts until the occupant leaves (`clearVolatile` ends with `setSpecies(baseSpecies)`: `sim/pokemon.ts:1508-1560`, called by the switch-out at `sim/battle-actions.ts:117` and by the faint at `sim/battle.ts:2563`) or changes forme: `setSpecies` calls `setType(species.types, true)` (`sim/pokemon.ts:1392`), and `runMegaEvo` goes through `formeChange` (`sim/battle-actions.ts:1902`, `sim/pokemon.ts:1427-1496`), so a Mega Evolution after Soak ends it, with no line; a Soak after the Mega Evolution takes (`g11_soak_mega`).
   - **State and rules.** The tail's `soak_type[roster]` (type id + 1; Water is 18) is the only state. `dfi_types_of` is the one place that reads a member's types: the forme's, or the Soak type alone. STAB (and Adaptability's doubling), the type chart (`dfi_type_immune`, `dfi_type_mod`), grounding (Grassy Terrain's heal and Grass boost, Psychic Terrain's priority block and boost, Grassy Glide's priority), the status immunities (burn on Fire, paralysis on Electric, freeze on Ice, poison on Poison and Steel) and Prankster's Dark rule all call it through the battle. The attacker's own types are unchanged by Soak on a foe: Soak on an ally changes the ally's STAB (`g11_soak_stab`). A move of the user that is stopped by Protect or a miss sets nothing. `dfi_run_mega` clears the soak type; `dfi_tail_clear_occupant` (a switch, a faint, a replacement) already did.
   - **A correction to the state rules (section 7).** The invariant `TAIL_MEMBER` said a soak type is only on a member that is not Mega Evolved, on the assumption that a Mega Evolution always follows Soak. A Mega Evolved Pokemon can be Soaked (`g11_soak_mega`, turn 3), so the rule is only "standing on the field": a weaker rule, no encoded state changes, no new revision. `test_pool_tail.c` had a case that expected the stricter rule (now valid), and the state model (`tools/state_model/state_v3_model.py`) the same.
   - **Event.** New public constant, owner's OK of 2026-10-02: `DUOFORGE_EVENT_TYPE_CHANGE` = 41 (POOL kinds): position, `detail` the new type id (`DUOFORGE_TYPE_*`; Soak: Water 17), `cause` MOVE with `id2` the move; shown to both players (the line is public). The converter reads `-start|X|typechange|TYPE` as one (a line with two types or an unknown type is an error: nothing else in the pool sets a type); with no `[from]` on the line the move is Soak, the one mechanic that sets a single type.
   - **View.** Decision 0018: `TYPE_CHANGED` and `type_now = {Water + 1, 0}` of the position of a Soaked occupant, supported bit 9, set by this step; the tracker's rule is `-start|X|typechange|TYPE` sets it, and a `|switch|`, `|drag|`, `|replace|`, `|faint|` or `|-mega|` of the position clears it (the research table of section 6.1 had OUT only; the Mega Evolution is added).
   - **Evidence.** Four recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g11_soak`, `g11_soak_mega`, `g11_soak_stab`, `g11_soak_electro`; `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` compares every event, HP and request with the reference, which is where the `-supereffective`, `-resisted` and `-immune` lines, the STAB through the HP that is left and the Grassy Terrain heal of the grounded Pelipper are read); `duoforge.state.pool_g11` reads the tail's soak type of every member and both viewers' whole extension after every step against rows that `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines; the interaction table of the pin is in that test's header. Mutation checks (the engine's read of the soak type, the clear on Mega Evolution, the clear on a switch, the pure-Water test, the view bit, the converter's line) each fail a test.
5c. **G7, Wide Guard** (the second mechanic on the tail; it consumes the `WIDE_GUARD` handler id, so the tables and the POOL fingerprints are unchanged). Pin: `data/moves.ts:20808-20851`: Rock status, priority +3, flags `snatch` only, target `allySide`, `sideCondition: 'wideguard'`; `onTry` (:20818) fails unless `queue.willAct()`; `onHitSide` (:20821-20823) gives the user the `stall` volatile (`addVolatile('stall')`: counter 3, or x3 up to 729 if it has one, duration 2, `data/conditions.ts:439-461`) and, unlike Protect (`onPrepareHit`, :13973-13975), runs no `StallMove` roll, so Wide Guard never fails by chance and raises the counter that a later Protect rolls against; the side condition (:20824-20846) has duration 1, `onSideStart` prints `-singleturn|user|Wide Guard` once (a second Wide Guard of the same side adds nothing and prints nothing but is no failure: `addSideCondition` returns false, `onHitSide`'s success is combined over it, `sim/battle-actions.ts:1240-1243, 1273-1275, 1561-1575`), and `onTryHit` at priority 4 (Protect's is 3) stops a move whose `target` is `allAdjacent` or `allAdjacentFoes` (the move's own target type: still stopped when only one target is left) unless `checkMoveBypassesProtect` (`sim/battle.ts:1300-1309`: the move has no `protect` flag, or an Unseen Fist) lets it through, whatever its category (a status spread move with the flag is stopped as well), with `-activate|target|move: Wide Guard` and NOT_FAIL. TryHit runs once for the whole target list, handlers sorted by priority and then by target (`sim/battle.ts:758-790, runEvent`), so every guarded target has its Wide Guard line first, a guarded target that also protects gets that line only, and the per-target result keeps the other targets going. Single-target moves (Sucker Punch, Fake Out, U-turn, Hurricane) and the foes' side are not touched; no spread move of the pool has positive priority, so Psychic Terrain (also 4) never ties with it; the tables have no `allAdjacent` move yet (Earthquake family is UNMODELED), when they do the ally hit is stopped too. Helping Hand, Follow Me and Fake Out have no interaction with it (Rage Powder is not in the pool); the priority +3 puts Wide Guard after Protect (+4) and before everything else, so Fake Out of a faster user can use up the last action before it and make it fail. State: the tail's per-side `wide_guard` (0/1): set by the move, cleared in the residual of the turn (a duration-1 side condition without an end line), live at a mid-turn pivot boundary. Events: `SINGLE_TURN` with id Wide Guard (position: the user), and `BLOCKED` with the new detail `DUOFORGE_BLOCK_WIDE_GUARD` = 4 (owner's OK 2026-10-02). View (decision 0018): `guard_flags` bit `WIDE_GUARD` of the guarded side for both viewers and the supported bit 17; the converter reads `-singleturn|X|Wide Guard` and `-activate|X|move: Wide Guard` (an empty `[spread] ` slot list is read as before). Evidence: four recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g7_wide_guard_a`: a guarded side with one Protect, both Hyper Voice and Snarl stopped per target, the Protects that roll against the raised counter; `_b`: a Wide Guard that fails with nobody left to act, Protect then Wide Guard then Protect, the counter 1/3 and 1/9; `_ally`: two Wide Guards of one side; `_pivot`: Wide Guard live when U-turn switches out mid-turn and the Hyper Voice after it stopped), `duoforge.state.pool_g7` (the side flag, the whole view extension of both viewers and the stall levels after every step, rows derived from the protocol lines by `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py`), mutation checks (no block, a Protect line as well, no willAct check, a singleturn line for the ally's too, no stall raise, a guard that is never cleared, a single-target move stopped, the foe's view hidden: all killed), and `duoforge.reference.diff_wide_guard_smoke` (twenty random battles of a team with Wide Guard, every request sampled for the domain check, a campaign of 400 more).
5d. **Sandstorm and Snowscape** (the weather step, the top of the M11 replay frequencies: Sand 6.45 percent and Snow 4.83 percent of the decisions). Sand Stream and Snow Warning join the weather-setter family (`DFI_FAMILY_WEATHER_SAND` 3 and `_SNOW` 4, the engine's `DFI_WEATHER_SAND` 3 and `_SNOW` 4; no Primal guard: the generator's pattern takes the optional guard of rain and sun only, and `pool_families.js` calls the pinned handlers with and without the orb), the moves Sandstorm and Snowscape become the two named handler ids `DFI_SPECIAL_SANDSTORM` and `_SNOWSCAPE` (the field `weather` has no column; each owns exactly `weather: 'Sandstorm'` resp. `'snowscape'`) and the type chart's `sandstorm: 3` becomes the immunity bit `DFI_IMMUNE_SAND` (32; Ground, Rock and Steel; the extended and closure canonical bytes mask it out), so the POOL fingerprints change (KP 18fcc0cc..., KPD f6a178b0... on the whole-pool tables). The generator reads the facts the engine hard-codes from the pin and fails on any other text (`check_weather_facts`: the conditions' duration 5, the 8 of the rock items, the stat boosts and their handler priority 10, the residual order 1, the damage `baseMaxhp / 16`, the upkeep and start lines; Snowscape has no `onWeather`; Weather Ball's types and doubling). Engine (`src/combat/turn.c`): the entry abilities and the moves set the weather for 5 turns (the same weather is not restarted: the ability does nothing, the move fails with `-fail` and `[still]`); Sandstorm damages every standing active Pokemon that is not Ground, Rock or Steel in `eachEvent` order (speed, a tie shuffled as a whole group, which the engine draws exactly when two of the tied Pokemon take damage, as the converter keeps the draws: `trace_to_c.drop_reason` for `each:Weather`, decided by the `[from] Sandstorm` damage lines of the step), `baseMaxhp / 16` (the cause `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_WEATHER` 16, id2 the weather value; generic for every weather that damages, in this format Sand only: Snow has no residual damage and Hail is isNonstandard "Past"), then the Update of eachEvent (Sitrus Berry), then the faints; this is the weather callback of the residual (order 1), before Grassy Terrain, Leftovers (5), poison (9) and burn (10). A Rock type has its Special Defense x1.5 in sand and an Ice type its Defense x1.5 in snow (`ModifySpD` and `ModifyDef` of priority 10, applied first); Weather Ball is a Rock move in sand and an Ice move in snow, doubled in every weather. The unmodelled sources of immunity or protection (Overcoat, Sand Force, Sand Rush, Sand Veil, Magic Guard) and of 8 turns (Smooth Rock, Icy Rock) stay unmarked, so no battle holds one (`duoforge.state.pool_weather` and `pool_families.js` check that). Marked: Sand Stream, Snow Warning, Sandstorm, Snowscape, by ten recorded battles (`w1_sand_stream`, `w2_sandstorm_move`, `w3_snow_warning`, `w4_snowscape_move`, `w5_sand_tie_four`, `_pairs` and `_mixed`, `w6_sand_residual_order`, `w7_sand_ko_sitrus`, and `w8_sand_soak`: Sandstorm and the Sand boost judge a Soaked Pokemon by its current type, step G11) and a differential campaign over a sand team and a snow team. View (decision 0018, supported bits 0 and 1): the new weather values are in the old observation's weather field; `duoforge.state.pool_weather` compares both viewers' weather and turns left with the `-weather` lines of the ten battles after every step (rows derived by `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py`) and the whole extension with its header. New public constants: `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_WEATHER` 16 (owner's OK); the weather values were declared by tier 0. The Python encoders 1 and 2 keep refusing weather 3 and 4 (ValueError); encoder 3 is the 0018 block.
5e. **G9, Encore** (the third mechanic on the tail: `last_move`, `encore_slot` and `encore_turns` of section 7, rev 1 as it is). Encore keeps its handler id (`DFI_SPECIAL_ENCORE`; no table change, so no fingerprint change) and the turn code implements it; it is marked.
   - **The pin.** `data/moves.ts:4724-4783`: a Normal status move, accuracy 100, flags protect, reflectable, mirror, bypasssub, metronome, failencore; condition `encore` with `duration: 3`, `onOverrideAction`, `onResidualOrder: 16` and `onDisableMove` (every slot but the stored move is disabled). The Champions mod replaces `onStart` (`data/mods/champions/moves.ts:309-345`): it fails (`-fail|user|[still]`: the move did nothing) without a `lastMove`, for a `failencore` move (Struggle and Encore are the only two with the flag) and for a stored move with 0 PP; otherwise `-start|target|Encore`, and when the target still has a move queued (`queue.willMove`) that is for another move and the target holds no Mental Herb, `queue.changeAction` replaces the action by the encored move. `addVolatile` refuses a target that has the volatile (there is no `onRestart`), which gives the same `-fail`. The duration is 3, or 4 when the target has no move queued (it has moved this turn, or is about to switch: the `+1` that the pin adds at `onStart`), counted down once per residual at order 16 (callback, so two holders at one Speed draw SPEED_TIE, which the sort entry `DFI_RES_ENCORE` takes part in); `-end|target|Encore` when the counter reaches 0 or the stored move has 0 PP. `Pokemon.lastMove` is the last move that the Pokemon itself used (Struggle included, stored as 5): the engine writes it where the move is used (`r->move_used`), under the POOL kinds only (the tail is all zero otherwise, `TAIL_KIND`). A switch-out, a faint or a replacement clears the tail position (`dfi_tail_clear_occupant`; a Mega Evolution does not, the volatile stays), so the Encore and the last move end silently with the occupant.
   - **The replacement and its draws.** `BattleQueue.changeAction` cancels the target's actions and `insertChoice` (`sim/battle-queue.ts:301-305`, `369-403`) puts the new action, which has no target, back: `resolveAction` first picks the target (`Battle.getRandomTarget`, `sim/battle.ts:2490-2522`: self, side and field moves target the user; an ally move the standing ally with one draw; otherwise `side.randomFoe()`, one RANDOM_TARGET draw `random(0, alive foes)`), then the action goes before the first queued action that it beats (`comparePriority`: order, priority with the user's ModifyPriority handlers, the user's action speed after `updateSpeed`); among queued actions that it ties it lands at `random(first, last + 1)`, a new draw site INSERT_TIE (`DFI_SITE_INSERT_TIE` = 14, drawn only when the range has more than one index). Every later sort in the turn recomputes every priority from the move and shuffles ties again (`sim/battle.ts:2921-2925`), so the place only matters for the tape: the engine takes the value from the reference and consumes it. The queue record of the replacement keeps the activation and the user, takes the new slot and the target, and has no reserve (the bench member that was chosen for a pivot move: the new action is built from the move alone).
   - **Request.** `dfi_slot_candidates` skips every move slot but the encored one while `encore_slot != 0` (a locked slot with 0 PP, or Fake Out disabled by the Champions rule after turn 1, leaves nothing and the slot gets Struggle: `g09_encore_struggle`); Choice locks and the other disables are applied as before, on the one slot that is left.
   - **Harness and converter.** `ps_trace.js` tested `insertChoice` before `getRandomTarget`, so Encore's random-target draw was recorded as INSERT_TIE/queue (team-gaps finding F5 a). The label is now RANDOM_TARGET (`resolve:insert`, or `action-speed` when `getActionSpeed` asked for it) when `getRandomTarget` is on the stack, INSERT_TIE only for the insertion's own draw; no committed trace holds a `changeAction`, so every older trace is byte for byte the same and the harness version stays 14. The converter's rules: the RANDOM_TARGET `resolve:insert` draw is kept (it decides the target, unlike the draws that only feed ModifyPriority); an INSERT_TIE is kept as a tape entry when its range holds moves and only move or residual actions; `-start|X|Encore` and `-end|X|Encore` become `VOLATILE_START` and `VOLATILE_END` (below); the `encore` volatile is ignored in the volatile lists; DisableMove tie groups accept the `H:encore:` entries. `test_trace_to_c.py` derives the expected slot of the lock per position and step from the protocol lines, and two tests pin the converter rules (a control without the rule is red).
   - **Events.** The two events of G8 are used with a new public detail value: `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_ENCORE` = 2 (`VOLATILE_START` and `VOLATILE_END`, detail 1 being Heal Block; owner approval of the value asked with the PR). Position of the target; both viewers see the line.
   - **View.** Decision 0018 section 6.1 and the extension: `position_ext.encore_slot` (slot + 1), supported bit 7 (`DUOFORGE_VIEWEXT_FEATURE_ENCORE`), set by this step; public to both viewers. No new field. The tracker's rule is the table row of section 6.1: `-start|X|Encore` sets it (to the slot of the last `|move|` of the occupant), `-end|X|Encore` and OUT clear it.
   - **Evidence.** Eight recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g09_encore_lock`: Encore before the target moves, the queued move is replaced with a random target, the requests offer one slot, the lock ends after three turns; `_late`: duration 4 for a target that has moved; `_fail`: no last move, the same move queued again (no replacement), Encore on an Encored target; `_encore`: Encore on a target whose last move is Encore (the failencore flag: `-fail`), and an Encore on a target that has moved (duration 4); `_switch`: the lock ends silently with the occupant; `_struggle`: Encore on Fake Out; `_tie_a`, `_tie_b`: a replaced action that ties an equal-Speed action, the two seeds being the two insertion outcomes, 0 and 1); `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` compares every event, HP and request and consumes the tape exactly, `duoforge.state.pool_g9` reads the tail and both viewers' extension after every step against rows that `test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines, plus the request domain under the lock, Struggle, the 0-PP early end and an Encore on a target whose last move is Struggle (state pokes, the second on the engine's own predicate) and the predicates against the pin's name list (which `tools/datagen/pool_families.js checkEncore` checks against the pin's text: duration 3, order 16, the flags and the callbacks). A campaign under the machine lock (120 battles, seed 9101, teams with four Encore users against the closure teams: 155 Encore starts in 73 battles, 89 `-end` lines) was 120 PASS. Mutation checks (no request lock, no replacement, a wrong duration, no late bonus, no residual entry, no INSERT_TIE draw, no RANDOM_TARGET draw, no view slot, the failencore list, the 0-PP end, the end event) each fail a test.
5f. **G17, the recharge moves** (the fourth mechanic on the tail: `must_recharge` of section 7, rev 2 as it is; M11 lists must recharge as #7 of the replay frequencies, Hyper Beam blocks 20 of the 227 survey teams). The seven recharge moves of the pool (Blast Burn, Frenzy Plant, Giga Impact, Hydro Cannon, Hyper Beam, Meteor Assault, Rock Wrecker) become data: a new bit of the second flags byte, `DFI_MOVE_FLAG2_RECHARGE` (8), which the generator reads from exactly the pinned text (`flags: {recharge: 1, ...}` with `self: { volatileStatus: 'mustrecharge', }`; one without the other is a refusal) and which takes the seven rows out of the UNMODELED rows (313 to 306). The POOL fingerprints change (`fc1d4a74...` for the tables, `9ef5db6a...` for POOL and `b6678904...` for POOL_DEV); CLOSURE and TEAM_C are byte for byte unchanged.
   - **The pin.** `data/conditions.ts:364-378`: `mustrecharge` has `duration: 2`, `onBeforeMovePriority: 11`, an `onBeforeMove` that shows `cant|X|recharge`, removes the volatile (and `truant`) and returns null, an `onStart` that shows `-mustrecharge|X` (`sim/SIM-PROTOCOL.md:603`) and `onLockMove: 'recharge'`. The ten moves with the flag (`data/moves.ts:1414-1429, 4936, 6242-6257, 6573-6588, 9055-9070, 9113-9128, 11723-11738, 13953, 15180, 15307-15322`; Meteor Assault has 170 power in the Champions mod, `data/mods/champions/moves.ts:635-639`) carry the volatile as a `self` effect, which `selfDrops` applies (`sim/battle-actions.ts:1096, 1317-1335`) for every target that was not ruled out: a miss, a Protect or an immunity make the target `false` and give no recharge; a knocked-out target is still a hit. A second `addVolatile` changes nothing, so one `-mustrecharge` line per move. `sim/pokemon.ts:964-972, 1084-1090, 1137`: the request of the next turn is the one move `Recharge`, the Pokemon trapped, no Mega; `sim/side.ts:575-583, 675-689` accept "move 1" and queue `{choice: 'move', moveid: 'recharge'}`. `runMove` (`sim/battle-actions.ts:210-264`) gets a random target for it (the name is no move: `getTarget` falls to `getRandomTarget`, one RANDOM_TARGET draw with two foes standing, kept as a tape entry like every other draw of that call; it decides nothing), asks `OverrideAction` (Encore, :227-235: an Encored Pokemon has the action turned into the Encored move and a second random target, a second draw), and `BeforeMove` ends it before PP are deducted and before `moveUsed`: no PP, no change of `lastMove`. The volatile ends with the occupant (`clearVolatile`, as for Soak).
   - **State and rules.** `must_recharge` (0 or 1) of the position is the only state; the turn code sets it after the hit loop's self step (`-mustrecharge` is the `VOLATILE_START` event with the detail `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_MUST_RECHARGE` = 3), `dfi_before_move` takes it first (priority 11, before sleep and freeze, flinch, Throat Chop, confusion and paralysis) for whatever move the action holds, shows `cant|X|recharge` (the `CANT` event with the cause `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_RECHARGE` = 18) and clears it. The recharge action is counted as a move action (Fake Out's rule counts it too: `activeMoveActions`). Encore on a Pokemon that must recharge replaces the queued recharge action by the Encored move, which then recharges all the same (the volatile is still there); an Encored Pokemon on its recharge turn has the override draw. Sucker Punch fails against a Pokemon with the volatile (`data/moves.ts:18405-18411`). **The residual sort.** `mustrecharge` has a duration and no callback, so it is an `end` handler of the residual's speed sort, as the stall counter is: the engine's list has one more duration entry per position that must recharge (`DFI_RES_PER_POSITION` is 11), because the swaps of the sort move tied entries and so decide the order of two Grassy Terrain heals at equal Speed (found by the first differential run, case `fz_9171_50`: a Hydro Cannon user and its tied ally).
   - **Request and choice (owner decision).** The slot of a Pokemon that must recharge is offered exactly one candidate: MOVE with `move_slot` = `DUOFORGE_MOVE_SLOT_RECHARGE` (5, next to Struggle's 4), no target, no Mega; no switch, no other move. `DUOFORGE_SLOT_PASS` keeps its meaning (an empty or fainted slot). The queue record of the action has the same move slot (the state checker allows it), and a decoded state with a queued move slot 5 is valid from now on (the tests that used 5 as the first invalid slot use 6).
   - **View.** Decision 0018: the `MUST_RECHARGE` bit of `position_ext.volatiles`, supported bit 15; public to both viewers (set by `-mustrecharge`, cleared by the `cant` line and by the occupant leaving). No new field.
   - **Converter and harness.** `-mustrecharge` and `cant|X|recharge` map to the two events above; the choice of a recharge turn (the state's `volatiles` has `mustrecharge`, Showdown's "move 1") is MOVE with slot 5; the reference's `enabled` row of such a request is the bit 0x20 (the recharge slot). The harness is unchanged (the state dump already lists `volatiles`), so no committed trace changes.
   - **Meteor Assault stays unmarked.** Its only learner, Sirfetch'd, has no supported ability (Steadfast and Scrappy), so no setup can use it; its row carries the bit and the Champions power (170) and the test says so. The survey team that has it is blocked by the ability first.
   - **Evidence.** Eleven recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g17_hyper_beam`: a hit, the recharge turn, the turn after; `_protect`: Protect stops it; `_immune`: a Ghost; `_miss`; `_ko`: a knocked-out target still gives it; `_encore`: Encore on a Pokemon that must recharge, and the recharge turn of an Encored Hyper Beam; `_sucker`: Sucker Punch fails against the recharging target and works as a control a turn later; `_flinch`: Fake Out on the recharge turn shows only `cant|recharge`; `_scarf`: a Choice Scarf holder is locked into Hyper Beam after its recharge; `g17_recharge_moves`: Blast Burn, Frenzy Plant, Hydro Cannon and Rock Wrecker recharge together; `g17_giga_impact`: Giga Impact and Hyper Beam together); `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` compares every event, HP, request and the tape (consumed exactly), `duoforge.state.pool_g17` reads the tail, the candidates of every slot and both viewers' extension after every step against rows that `test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines, and refuses another move, a switch or a pass for the recharging slot. `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` checks the pinned facts (the ten moves, the condition, Sucker Punch's `onTry`). The differential loop under the machine lock (teams of Hyper Beam, Giga Impact, Hydro Cannon, Blast Burn, Sucker Punch, Fake Out and Encore users against the closure teams and each other): the first run (100 battles, seed 9171) found one divergence, fixed here and promoted into the corpus (`fz_9171_50_prefix`); the fixed engine then ran 100 battles (seed 9171) and 200 (seed 9172, with Encore teams): all PASS, with 1045 `-mustrecharge` lines and 894 `cant|X|recharge` lines in 268 battles, and the engine offered what Showdown accepts in all 1430 sampled requests. Mutation checks (no volatile, set without a hit, a knocked-out target not a hit, a recharge flag ignored for one move, no start event, no cant event, the volatile not cleared, the recharge check after the flinch, no recharge candidate in the request, no view bit, no base random-target draw, no Encore override draw, a recharge action that resets the move counter) each fail a test; one survivor, Sucker Punch's own `mustrecharge` test next to the queue-record test, is unreachable (an Encore that replaces the recharge action acts after Sucker Punch, whose priority is +1) and is kept because the pin has it.
5g. **G14, Rough Skin, Poison Touch and Thermal Exchange** (three abilities that the turn code runs by id: they are `ENGINE_ROWS` of the generator, so their handler column is NONE and the POOL fingerprints change with the table hash; no family, no public value: the damage and the status use the existing `CAUSE_ABILITY` with the holder or the attacker as `other`, and the boost the Stamina path). Pin (the Champions mod has no override of any of them): **Rough Skin** `data/abilities.ts:3938-3950`: `onDamagingHitOrder: 1`, so it runs before Rocky Helmet (order 2, `data/items.ts:5295-5309`) and before every unordered handler (Stamina, Thermal Exchange, the attacker's Poison Touch); `checkMoveMakesContact` (`sim/battle.ts:1289-1298`: the contact flag, unless the attacker holds Protective Pads, which the pool does not have; Long Reach, which would delete the flag from its holder's moves, is unmarked) then `damage(source.baseMaxhp / 8, source, target)`: truncated, at least 1 (`spreadDamage`, `sim/battle.ts:2091-2150`; `Pokemon.damage`), the line `-damage|attacker|hp|[from] ability: Rough Skin|[of] holder`, also when the hit knocked the holder out, and the attacker's Emergency Exit sees it like Rocky Helmet's. Iron Barbs has the same entry (`data/abilities.ts:2224-2235`) but is not in the legal pool, so it has no row. **Poison Touch** `data/abilities.ts:3370-3383`: `onSourceDamagingHit` (the attacker's handler, appended after the target's own handlers, `sim/battle.ts:1035-1063`, the sort `compareLeftToRightOrder` is stable): for a contact move (`checkMoveMakesContact(move, target, source)`) `randomChance(3, 10)` (one `random(10)`, a new draw site `POISON_TOUCH` 16 of the tape, named by `ps_trace.js` through the effect and the event: no committed trace had one, the harness version stays 14) and then `trySetStatus('psn', source)`: `[-status] psn [from] ability: Poison Touch [of] attacker` (`data/conditions.ts` psn `onStart`); the roll is drawn also for a Poison or Steel type and for a target that is down (the handler runs first, the status fails after); Shield Dust and Covert Cloak, which the pin lets block it, are not marked; Sheer Force does not touch it (it is not a secondary). **Thermal Exchange** `data/abilities.ts:4990-5018`: `onDamagingHit`: a Fire move (`move.type`, after an -ate or Weather Ball change) raises the holder's Attack by 1 (`-ability|holder|Thermal Exchange|boost`, `-boost`: the Stamina path), also for each target of a spread Heat Wave; `onSetStatus` refuses every burn (a move's own burn shows `-immune|holder|[from] ability: Thermal Exchange`, a secondary such as Flare Blitz's or an item's burn shows nothing, `sim/pokemon.ts:1724` and `data/abilities.ts:5003-5010`; the secondary's roll is drawn all the same); its `onUpdate` cure of a burn that a holder already has is unreachable (a member starts without a status and the ability blocks every burn). With a Flower Veil holder in play the order of the two SetStatus refusals is `E_UNSUPPORTED`. Evidence: recorded POOL battles `g14_rough_skin` (Rough Skin and Rocky Helmet on one hit, a Rough Skin hit that knocks the holder out, a non-contact move), `g14_poison_touch` (a roll that poisons, rolls at a target that is down and at one that is poisoned), `g14_poison_touch_faint` (the roll is drawn after the attacker was knocked out by Rough Skin and Rocky Helmet damage, and at a target that is down: confirmed against the reference) and `g14_thermal_exchange` (the boost per Fire hit, a spread Heat Wave, a burn roll of 4 that is refused without a line); the converter treats Thermal Exchange's onUpdate as no holder of an `each:Update` speed tie (precondition: the holder is not burned, checked on the state), found by a campaign; mutation checks (no Rough Skin, /6 instead of /8, the roll threshold, no roll at a target that is down, the Fire test, the burn refusal: all killed); campaigns of 300 and 1000 random battles of a team with the three abilities, all PASS, 8012 sampled requests agree with Showdown; `duoforge.reference.diff_replay`. Not marked in this step and why: Iron Barbs (not in the pool); Long Reach, Shield Dust (pin interactions above) and Sheer Force stay UNMODELED rows.
5h. **G16, Knock Off** (19 of the 227 survey teams; the first user of the tail's `item_now`, rev 2). Knock Off keeps its handler id as a row of the whole pool, and the turn code implements it; Sticky Hold, which it reads by id, becomes an engine row. Both are marked, with the view bit 11.
   - **The pin.** `data/moves.ts:9959-9984` (the Champions mod does not override the move): Dark, Physical, 65, accuracy 100, contact, protect, mirror. `onBasePower` (priority 0, so the last handler of the chain: after Fairy Aura 20, the type items 15, Helping Hand 10 and the terrains 6) chains 1.5 when `singleEvent('TakeItem', item, ...)` of the target's item answers true and the item exists; the item's own `onTakeItem` is all that is asked there (Sticky Hold is not: a Swalot holding an item is hit for the boosted power). `onAfterHit` is `target.takeItem()` and, when it gave an item, `-enditem|target|Item|[from] move: Knock Off|[of] user`. **The Champions mod runs the after-hit without asking whether the user still stands** (`data/mods/champions/scripts.ts:411-415`; the base game has `&& pokemon.hp` at `sim/battle-actions.ts:1123`, and the research note of the survey, `champions-reg-mc-report-draft.md:211`, says so): a user that the Rocky Helmet knocked out takes the item all the same (`g16_helmet_faint`). The after-hit runs for every damaged target, a target that this hit knocked out included (its faint is not processed yet), after `DamagingHit` (Rocky Helmet's damage, Stamina) and before the Emergency Exit check.
   - **`takeItem` and `TakeItem`** (`sim/pokemon.ts:1851-1866`; `sim/battle.ts:956-972`, the ability's handler before the item's): no item, nothing (a Sitrus Berry already eaten, a Focus Sash that this very hit used up, an item taken before: no boost and no line). Sticky Hold (`data/abilities.ts:4622-4635`) of a Pokemon that is **alive** shows `-activate|X|ability: Sticky Hold` and the item stays; of a fainted one it does not block, so a Swalot that the hit knocked out loses its Leftovers (`g16_sticky_hold`). Unburden's `onTakeItem` (`data/abilities.ts:5240-5242`) adds its volatile at this point. The item's own `onTakeItem`: the **only** items of the pool that have one are the 81 Mega Stones, each of which answers false for its own species (`!item.megaStone?.[source.baseSpecies.baseSpecies]`; Floettite and Meowsticite compare by name, with the same result; the source of `takeItem()` is the Pokemon itself), so a Mega Stone on its own species is neither boosted nor taken, before and after the Mega Evolution (`g16_stones`), and a Mega Stone on another species is taken like any item (`g16_removal`, Charizardite Y on a Sneasler). No mask, plate, drive, orb or Z crystal is in the pool. Neither the item's `End` nor `AfterTakeItem` has a handler in the pool.
   - **State.** `item_now` of the member (decision 0018, section 7 here) is the one item the engine reads: `dfi_item_code(b, m)` in `turn.c` is its accessor, as `dfi_ability_code` is for the ability (the sheet's item unless it was used up, or the tail's: 255 is "holds nothing", 1 to 254 an item id + 1, which nothing writes yet). Every rule that read `dfi_member.item` reads it: the Choice Scarf's Speed and lock, Life Orb, Rocky Helmet, Light Clay, Leftovers, White Herb, Sitrus Berry, Grassy Seed, the type boosters and resist berries and Focus Sash (the pure functions of `combat/item_family.h` take the item code now, `tests/test_item_family.c` follows with the same cases). A taken item is gone **for good**: `item_now` stays across a switch-out and a faint (`sim/pokemon.ts:1851-1866` restores nothing; the only trace of the old rule, `itemKnockedOff`, is read for generation 4 alone), which is what the tail comment said; `dfi_tail_clear_occupant` does not touch it (it clears the position, the soak type, the current ability and the toxic stage). A Pokemon that comes back in has no item and no Unburden volatile (the volatile ends with the position).
   - **Rules of the turn.** The boost is the last link of the BasePower chain. The removal comes from `dfi_knock_off` after the DamagingHit handlers, for every hit target in order: Sticky Hold (alive) shows its `-activate` and stops; a Mega Stone on its own species stops silently (`mega_capable`: exactly "holds the stone of its own base forme", kept after the Mega Evolution); otherwise `item_now` becomes 255 and ITEM_END shows it. An Unburden holder that is alive gets its volatile (Speed x2: in `g16_unburden` the Sneasler moves before the Meowscarada that was faster from the next turn on). **A Choice lock ends with the item** (`choicelock`'s `onBeforeMove` and `onDisableMove` remove it for a Pokemon without a Choice item, and a move that was already chosen still runs, so ending the lock here is the same): the next request offers every move, and the lost Scarf is no longer in the Speed (`g16_scarf_helmet`). A Sitrus Berry is taken before the `Update` that would eat it (`g16_removal`: the hit leaves Pelipper at half or less and nothing is eaten, nothing heals). The Unburden volatile of a holder whose stone then refuses (Hawlucha with Hawluchanite) was the one place where the pin and the engine differed in what is set; the Mega batch (5n), which marks Hawluchanite, sets it as the pin does.
   - **Invariants.** A choice lock needs the Choice item that the member still has (it said "no item is ever lost while it holds"); Unburden's volatile needs the item gone, used up or taken.
   - **The lock outlives the item until the pin ends it (a conformance fix of this step's first reading).** Knock Off took the lock with the Scarf at once; the pin does not: choicelock ends in its own `onBeforeMove` (`data/conditions.ts:332-336`, the holder's next move, when it holds no Choice item) and `onDisableMove` (`:349-352`, run for every active Pokemon in `endTurn`, `sim/battle.ts:1691`); a Choice item's own `onStart` ends it when the holder gets one (`data/items.ts:966-972`). So a stop in the middle of a turn after a holder that has moved lost its Scarf (a replacement, a pivot, the end of the battle) still shows the lock. The engine: `dfi_choice_lock_ends` in `dfi_run_move` (after BeforeMove let the holder move) and in `dfi_end_turn`, nowhere else; the invariant (`invariants.c`) accepts a lock without its Choice item under the POOL kinds at every boundary but the turn's. Found by the first mirror campaign of step G29 (30 of 1200 battles; 4 with Knock Off alone); the recorded battle `g16_scarf_lock_stays` shows it. No version bump: no recorded battle or golden changed.
   - **Event (new public value, owner's OK of 2026-10-02).** `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_ITEM_TAKEN` = 17, a cause of ITEM_END: the item was taken by a move; `id` the move, `other` the user, `id2` item + 1; the old events of an item used up are unchanged. The old `item_used` of the view shows the item gone for a taken item as for a used-up one (the reference's own state does, and the foe's knowledge flag follows the event; the invariant of the knowledge accepts a taken item), `item_now` tells them apart. The converter reads `-enditem|X|Item|[from] move: M|[of] Y` as one and refuses any other shape of a `[from] move:` line; `-activate|X|ability: Sticky Hold` is the ACTIVATE of the ability, as every other one.
   - **Deviation from the plan sent to the coordinator (owner OK, 2026-10-02: under POOL the base view's consumed flag may also mean "taken by Knock Off").** The plan left the base view's `item_used` alone. It is changed: the consumed flag of the base view now also means "taken", because the reference's own state shows a taken item as gone in `item_used`. In the code, `events.c` marks the member's item consumed (the foe's knowledge) for an ITEM_END of any cause, and the knowledge invariant accepts `item_consumed == 1` or `item_now == NONE` (255). The ext overlay `item_now` (NONE) is what tells a taken item from a used-up one. Used up or taken, the old events of an item used up are unchanged. This is POOL-only in practice: an ITEM_END with the cause ITEM_TAKEN only exists there (Knock Off is marked in no other kind).
   - **View.** Decision 0018: `item_now` = 255 (`DUOFORGE_ITEM_NOW_NONE`) of the member, public (the `-enditem` line is in both streams), both sides' roster, supported bit 11 `ITEM_CHANGE`. **Trick, Switcheroo and Thief stay unmarked**, so setup refuses them and no accepted battle has a swapped item: `item_now` is 0 or 255 in every battle that runs, and the bit is set for the item that a move took alone (as G8 set Heal Block's bit with Psychic Noise alone).
   - **Tables.** The special id KNOCK_OFF (24) is new, so UNMODELED moves to 25 and every UNMODELED move row changes by a byte: the pool tables, their hash and the POOL fingerprints change (CLOSURE and TEAM_C do not). The generator checks the whole text of both callbacks (whitespace aside) against the pin, and `ENGINE_ROWS` has Sticky Hold. 305 moves and 180 abilities are unmodeled (306 and 181 before).
   - **Evidence.** Six recorded battles under the POOL kind (genders stated; seeds found by predicates over recorded battles): `g16_removal` (Pelipper's Sitrus Berry taken at 38 percent, not eaten and no heal; Sneasler's Charizardite Y taken; Sneasler switches out and comes back with nothing; the Gholdengo that comes in loses its Life Orb; Knock Off at an empty hand has the plain power and no line), `g16_unburden` (Sneasler's Sitrus Berry taken: the next turn it moves before the Meowscarada that was faster; a Gholdengo's Life Orb), `g16_stones` (Staraptite and Salamencite stay, also after the Mega Evolution), `g16_sticky_hold` (Swalot's Sticky Hold alive: -activate and the Leftovers stay; a Raichu's Focus Sash used by the hit and nothing taken after it; the Meowscarada's own Black Glasses taken by the Swalot's Knock Off; the knocked-out Swalot loses its Leftovers), `g16_scarf_helmet` (Annihilape's Choice Scarf goes with its lock, Archaludon's Rocky Helmet hurts the user first and goes) and `g16_helmet_faint` (the user that the helmet knocks out takes the helmet all the same). `duoforge.state.pool_g16` reads the tail's `item_now` of every member, the events of the step (counts, move, user, item) and both viewers' whole extension after every step against rows that `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines (the `-enditem ... Knock Off` and `-activate ... Sticky Hold` lines); the invariants are checked on states of the battles. Mutation checks of the implementation, 26 edits, each fails `duoforge.state.pool_g16` or the conformance: the boost dropped, given to an own stone or to an empty hand; Sticky Hold ignored, silent, or blocking a fainted holder; an own stone taken; Unburden not set on removal or set on a fainted holder; the lock kept; `item_now` not written; the removal needing a standing user or skipping a fainted target; the cause, the move or the user of the event dropped; the accessor ignoring the tail; the Scarf Speed, the Leftovers heal, the Rocky Helmet or the Sitrus Berry reading the sheet instead of the accessor; a switch-out giving the item back; the foe's knowledge not following the event; the view without `item_now`; the Unburden and the Choice lock invariants loosened.
5i. **G15, Psychic Terrain completion** (the two parts that blocked the most teams: Expanding Force 37 of 227, Psychic Seed 30). Pin (Champions mod, which overrides neither entry): **Expanding Force** `data/moves.ts:4943-4965`: Psychic, Special, 80 base power, accuracy 100, priority 0, flags `protect`, target `normal`; its own `onBasePower` (:4952-4957) is `chainModify(1.5)` and its `onModifyMove` (:4958-4962) sets `move.target = 'allAdjacentFoes'`, both only when `this.field.isTerrain('psychicterrain') && source.isGrounded()`. Order: `useMoveInner` (`sim/battle-actions.ts:431-443`) runs ModifyMove after `runMove`'s `getTarget` and BeforeMove, so the target is chosen and drawn as for a `normal` move (the request offers a target, an unavailable chosen foe is retargeted at random, a chosen ally is kept), and only then does the class change: `getRandomTarget` is called again (its draw only labels the move line, as for any spread move, and the converter drops it: `RANDOM_TARGET execute:allAdjacentFoes`), and `getMoveTargets` (`sim/pokemon.ts:791-810`) collects every adjacent foe, skipping the redirection of its default branch (Follow Me and Lightning Rod do not redirect the spread version, and do the single-target one). The spread modifier is 0.75 when more than one target is left (`sim/battle-actions.ts:551`, :1733-1737), so with one foe standing the move is a single-target hit with the same x1.5. Wide Guard (G7) and the protect check read `move.target` of the active move, so Wide Guard stops the spread version only. The x1.5 is the move's own BasePower handler, which `runEvent` puts first and sorts with the others by priority (0 for a handler that has none: after Helping Hand's 10, the items' 15 and the terrain's 6), the chain rounding once. `isGrounded` (`sim/pokemon.ts:2148-2160`): the engine reads the Flying type alone; Levitate, Eelevate, Air Balloon, Iron Ball, Gravity, Ingrain, Magnet Rise, Smack Down and Roost are UNMODELED rows (Telekinesis is not in the format), and no forme that learns Expanding Force is a Flying type, so a lifted user cannot occur (`test_pool_rows.py` checks both); the row of a user that is not grounded is therefore not a recordable case. **Psychic Seed** `data/items.ts:4903-4922` is Grassy Seed (`:2595-2614`) for Psychic Terrain and the Special Defense: `onSwitchInPriority: -1`, `onStart` (not `ignoringItem` and the terrain is up) and `onTerrainChange` (the terrain is up) call `useItem`, `boosts: { spd: 1 }`; the generator checks that text (`check_g15_facts`) and the engine reads one rule for both seeds (`dfi_terrain_seed`, `src/combat/turn.c`): the entry abilities (priority 0, Psychic Surge included) run before the seeds of the same switch-in, a terrain started by the ability or the move runs every seed on the field in `eachEvent` order (a tie of equal-Speed holders is kept: the order of their lines), a seed that waits for the other terrain stays, and a Contrary holder gets -1. Electric Seed and Misty Seed stay UNMODELED (no Electric or Misty Terrain). The Psychic Terrain move itself is still an UNMODELED row (its condition has no column; Psychic Surge sets the terrain in every battle here). Tables: `EXPANDING_FORCE` is a new named handler id (`G15_HANDLERS` of the generator, after the weather ids and before UNMODELED, so the POOL table hash and the fingerprints change as for every pool step) and Psychic Seed an `ENGINE_ROWS` item; both are marked. `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` pins that every modelled ability or item with a priority or Speed callback is Prankster, Unburden or Choice Scarf (an addition of the fainted-holder fix), and `test_pool_rows.py` that nothing that grounds or lifts a Pokemon is modelled. No new public value, no new event, no view change (the seed's lines are the Grassy Seed's: `-enditem` and `-boost` with `[from] item`). Evidence: eleven recorded POOL battles (`g15_ef_chain`: the spread in the terrain with the 0.75 modifier, the chain Twisted Spoon (15), Helping Hand (10), terrain (6), move (0), a Protect that stops one target of the spread, a resisted spread; `g15_ef_no_terrain`: the single-target move, not stopped by Wide Guard; `g15_ef_wide_guard`: the spread version stopped for both targets by both users, the next turn hit again; `g15_ef_follow_me`: Follow Me redirects the single-target move and not the spread one; `g15_ef_retarget_terrain` and `_plain`: the chosen target faints first, with one foe left the move is a single hit with or without the terrain; `g15_seed_surge`, `_terrain_starts`, `_tie` (the equal-Speed order of two seeds is drawn), `_contrary_unburden` and `_other_terrain`), the mutation checks (each of the following changes makes `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` fail and no other test: no x1.5, no spread class, Wide Guard reading the base class, the seed boosting the wrong stat, Psychic Seed not recognised, the seed not used at the switch-in; Follow Me reading the base class is an equivalent mutant: the only foe left is then the Follow Me user itself), `duoforge.reference.tiebreak_pool_data`, a differential campaign (seed 151, 1500 battles over a Psychic Terrain team against itself, both reference teams and Team C: 1500 PASS, 5260 requests sampled for the domain check, all agreed) and the generator, row and fact tests.
5j. **G18, four Mega Stones** (Tyranitarite 15 of the 227 survey teams, Baxcalibrite 13, Aerodactylite 1, Manectite 1; no engine rule and no public value: the Mega path is generic, the step marks four stones in the manifest and records what each Mega does). A stone is marked when the Mega forme's ability is already marked, a base forme of the holder has a marked ability, and the base forme has exactly one Mega. Nothing in the tables changes (the stones are pure data: their only callback is the `onTakeItem` of every Mega Stone, `STONE_TAKE_ITEM`).
   - **The pin.** No Champions-specific rule for any of the stones: `data/mods/champions/items.ts` has `inherit: true, isNonstandard: null` only (Tyranitarite :978, Baxcalibrite :82, Aerodactylite and Manectite likewise); the base items are `data/items.ts:7392` (Tyranitarite, `megaStone: {Tyranitar: Tyranitar-Mega}`, `itemUser`, `onTakeItem`) and :403 (Baxcalibrite). `canMegaEvo` of the mod (`data/mods/champions/scripts.ts:182-194`) returns `item.megaStone[baseSpecies.name]`, and the mod's `formeChange` (:56-109) keeps a Mega after it faints, prints `-mega` and counts as the Truant action. The mod has no `pokedex.ts`: the formes are those of `data/pokedex.ts`: Tyranitar-Mega (:4961-4974) Rock and Dark, 100/164/150/95/120/71, Sand Stream, 255 kg; Baxcalibur-Mega (:19056-19069) Dragon and Ice, 115/175/117/105/101/87, Thermal Exchange, 315 kg; Aerodactyl-Mega (:3190-3204) Rock and Flying, 80/135/85/70/95/150, Tough Claws, 79 kg (base :3176, Rock Head / Pressure / Unnerve); Manectric-Mega (:5952-5965) Electric, 70/75/80/135/80/135, Intimidate, 44 kg (base :5937, Static / Lightning Rod / Minus). `setAbility` (`sim/pokemon.ts:1908-1946`) ends the old ability, starts the Mega's own with a fresh state and runs its Start event even when it is the same ability (:1941-1944: only a Transform of the same ability does not): Sand Stream sets the weather again unless it is the same weather, Intimidate lowers the foes' Attack at the Mega Evolution, Thermal Exchange and Tough Claws have no Start.
   - **What runs where.** `dfi_run_mega` (turn.c) emits the forme line and the Mega line, then `dfi_entry_ability` for an ability with an entry effect (weather, terrain, Intimidate, Fairy Aura); the setup gate (`dfi_manifest_mega`, closure_member.c) needs the Mega ability and the stone marked. All four Mega abilities are marked (Sand Stream, Thermal Exchange since G14, Tough Claws, Intimidate); the base abilities that the recorded sets use are Sand Stream, Thermal Exchange, Rock Head and Lightning Rod (all marked).
   - **Not marked, and why.** Charizardite X (3 teams), Garchompite Z (15), Lucarionite Z (11), Absolite Z (5) and Raichunite X need more than a mark: **the tables link one Mega per base forme** (the generator takes the first Mega of the legal pool's order: gen_closure.py:2159-2163; Charizard links Mega-Y, Garchomp Mega, Lucario Mega, Absol Mega), read by closure_member.c (146-149, 254-258, 334-337, 387-399, 425-431), turn.c (104, 3750) and the data API (data_query.c:73-92); a recorded battle of Charizardite X failed with E_INVALID_ARGUMENT at the Mega choice (the holder is not "holding its own stone"), and the tables already carry "second Mega forme" as an unmodelled feature of those stones, so the half-modelled check refuses a mark. Their step is a model of Mega by stone (decision to follow, with Levitate for Garchomp-Mega-Z). Also unmarked, by the base forme or the Mega ability: Gardevoirite (Pixilate is marked, but Synchronize, Trace and Telepathy are not; follow-up once Trace lands), Froslassite (Snow Warning is marked, Snow Cloak and Cursed Body are not), Metagrossite (Tough Claws marked; Clear Body and Light Metal not), Glimmoranite (Adaptability marked; Toxic Debris and Corrosion not), Lucarionite Z (Aura Guard is unmodelled and Inner Focus is not marked), and Mega abilities that nothing models: Absolite Z Sharpness, Gengarite Shadow Tag, Swampertite Swift Swim, Blastoisinite Mega Launcher, Blazikenite Speed Boost, Dragoninite Multiscale, Pyroarite Fire Mane, Scizorite Technician, Alakazite and Meowsticite Trace, Cameruptite Sheer Force.
   - **Evidence.** Five recorded battles under the POOL kind (genders stated; seed 1 for all; the Mega Evolution happens in every one and the Mega ability acts): `g18_tyranitar_replaces` (Sand Stream at the start; the foe's Pelipper comes in in turn 2 and Drizzle replaces the sand; then Tyranitar Mega Evolves and the Mega's Sand Stream replaces the rain, with the sand chipping Pelipper), `g18_tyranitar_same` (the Mega Evolution at turn 3 while the first sand is up: no second weather line and the sand ends on the turn that the first Sand Stream gave it), `g18_baxcalibur_thermal` (Thermal Exchange before the Mega: Flamethrower gives Attack +1; after the Mega Evolution, the turn it uses Protect, and then a Flamethrower and a Flare Blitz that each give +1; no line at the Mega Evolution), `g18_aerodactyl` (Rock Head becomes Tough Claws: Iron Head before and after, a Rock Slide with no contact), `g18_manectric` (Lightning Rod draws a Thunderbolt before the Mega; the Mega's Intimidate lowers Raichu and Kingambit, and Kingambit's Defiant answers). 125 pool battles and 672 stops (120 and 644 before), 47 marked moves as before; four items marked.
   - **Mutation checks.** Ten edits, each killed by the conformance or the pool tables test: no entry ability at the Mega Evolution, the weather restarted when it is the same, the Mega keeping the base ability, the Mega keeping the base stats, the forme line naming the base, no Intimidate entry at the Mega, and each of the four stones unmarked.
5k. **G19, Coaching and Glaive Rush** (17 and 15 of the 227 survey teams; two mechanics that share no engine code, one PR because both change the generated pool tables). The handler column gets one new id, `DFI_SPECIAL_GLAIVE_RUSH` (the id of UNMODELED moves up by one), the boost-role column one new value, `DFI_BOOST_ROLE_PRIMARY_ALLY` (4, internal, defined in `pool_tables.h`); the POOL fingerprints change (`38556ca8...` for the tables, `a1dc59ca...` for POOL and `2a46ebda...` for POOL_DEV), CLOSURE and TEAM_C do not. Two rows leave UNMODELED (305 to 302 with the move that G16 left). No public value changes: nothing of either move is a new event, cause, draw site or choice.
   - **Coaching, the pin.** `data/moves.ts:2590-2605`: Status, accuracy true, PP 10, flags `bypasssub`, `allyanim` and `metronome` only (no `protect`: Protect does not stop it), target `adjacentAlly`, `boosts: {atk: 1, def: 1}`; the Champions mod has no entry. The generator gives a status move with that target and primary boosts the role `PRIMARY_ALLY` (the same boosts on any other non-self target stay refused). The turn code runs it after the target step that Helping Hand uses: with no standing ally there is no target (`[notarget]`, `-fail`), a Good as Gold ally is immune (`-immune|ally|[from] ability: Good as Gold`: a status move of another Pokemon), and otherwise the ordinary boost path (`dfi_boost`, `sim/battle.ts:2020-2088`) raises the ally: Contrary or a cap at the ally change it, Defiant and Competitive answer only a drop that a foe causes (`source` on the other side), Flower Veil blocks only drops. `-boost|ally|atk|1`, `-boost|ally|def|1`, no `[from]`.
   - **Glaive Rush, the pin.** `data/moves.ts:6647-6678`: Physical, 120, accuracy 100, PP 5, `self: {volatileStatus: 'glaiverush'}` and a condition: `onStart` shows `-singlemove|X|Glaive Rush|[silent]` (the line is in the log with the `[silent]` flag, which a client does not display; the converter drops silent lines and the engine has no event for it), `onAccuracy` returns true, `onSourceModifyDamage` returns `chainModify(2)`, `onBeforeMovePriority: 100`, `onBeforeMove` removes the volatile. The Champions mod has only its learnset (Baxcalibur). The self effect is `selfDrops`' (`sim/battle-actions.ts:1096, 1317-1335`), so as for the recharge: a hit gives the volatile, a miss, a Protect or an immunity do not. The holder of an `onSource...` handler is the defender (the event's source): the moves that hit the user cannot miss (the `Accuracy` event, `sim/battle-actions.ts:736-738`: `true` means no draw, so the engine has no ACCURACY draw for them) and do twice the damage (`ModifyDamage`: `chainModify(2)`, 8192/4096). The user's next action of any kind starts with the removal (priority 100, before flinch 8, the recharge 11 and everything else), so it is gone when that action is a flinch, a recharge turn or a move that then fails; leaving the field or fainting ends it too. The user stays vulnerable for the rest of the turn it used the move and until its next action begins: a faster foe on the next turn hits it doubled, a slower one after it does not.
   - **State and view.** `glaive_rush` (0 or 1) of the position is the state (tail rev 2, nothing new); the view is the existing `GLAIVE_RUSH` bit of `position_ext.volatiles`, supported bit 20 (decision 0018 section 6.1: set by the move line that hit, cleared by the user's next `move` or `cant` line and by leaving). The silent line is no event; the view bit carries it.
   - **The one case not modelled.** The ModifyDamage modifiers are chained from 4096 with a rounding at each step; Life Orb (5324), a resist berry (2048) and a screen (2732) commute in every order, and so does the doubling with any two of them, but all four together give 3552 or 3551 by their order (decided by the holders' speeds, and a tie draw). That combination (a Life Orb attacker, a resist berry eaten, a screen, and a Glaive Rush target) is refused with `E_UNSUPPORTED`, not guessed. The converter drops a ModifyDamage tie of Glaive Rush with the other modifiers (all subsets of at most three commute) under that precondition.
   - **Evidence.** Ten recorded battles under the POOL kind: `g19_coaching` (+1/+1 for an ally with Defiant, twice; Protect does not matter), `_contrary` (Serperior is lowered), `_good_as_gold`, `_no_ally` (the ally was knocked out earlier in the turn: `[notarget]`, `-fail`), `_protect`, `g19_glaive_rush` (turn 2: Raichu hits the user doubled and cannot miss, Milotic, slower than the user, hits it normally and misses; turn 3 normal), `_protect` and `_immune` (no drawback: the foe's Muddy Water can miss), `_flinch` (Fake Out the turn after: doubled, `cant|flinch`, the drawback is gone for the slower foe), `_mega` (Mega Baxcalibur, step G18: Glaive Rush on the turn it Mega Evolves). `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` compares every event, HP, request and the tape; `duoforge.state.pool_g19` reads the tail and both viewers' extension after every step against rows that `test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines; `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` checks the pinned facts of both moves. Mutation checks (the volatile not set, set without a hit, never cleared, cleared only without a flinch, the accuracy draw kept, no doubling, tripling, no view bit, Coaching boosting its user, Coaching ignoring Good as Gold) each fail a test. Two differential campaigns under the machine lock (teams of Baxcalibur, Sneasler, Raichu, Serperior, Gholdengo and Incineroar against the closure teams, the recharge and Encore teams and themselves; 400 battles, seeds 9191 and 9192): all PASS, with 322 Coaching moves and 595 Glaive Rush moves (435 hits) in 310 battles, and the engine offered what Showdown accepts in all 1562 sampled requests.
5l. **G20, Aurora Veil** (2.28 percent of the replay decisions, decision 0018 tier 1; the first mechanic that fills `aurora_veil_turns` of the tail, rev 2 as it is). Aurora Veil keeps a handler id of its own (`AURORA_VEIL`: its `onTry` and the side condition that no column holds), and the turn code implements it; it is marked with the view bit 3.
   - **The pin.** `data/moves.ts:830-877` (the Champions mod does not override it): Ice, Status, PP 20, flags snatch and metronome, `sideCondition: 'auroraveil'`. `onTry` (:840) returns `field.isWeather(['hail', 'snowscape'])`: the move fails with `-fail` and `[still]` outside snow (hail is not in the format; nothing suppresses a weather here, Cloud Nine and Air Lock are unmarked). `durationCallback` (:842-847): 8 turns when the user holds Light Clay, else 5; `addSideCondition` does not restart an active condition, so a second cast fails the same way. `onAnyModifyDamage` (:848-860) for a target of the holder's side other than the attacker returns without an effect when the side has Reflect and the move is Physical or Light Screen and it is Special, and otherwise, unless the hit is a critical hit (or `infiltrates`, which no marked move or ability has), chains 2732/4096 with two Pokemon per side. So a hit is reduced once, by Aurora Veil or by the screen of its category. `onSideStart` and `onSideEnd` print `-sidestart|side|move: Aurora Veil` and `-sideend|...`; the end is in the residual at order 26, sub-order 10, after Reflect (1), Light Screen (2) and Tailwind (5). The generator reads the `onTry` and the whole condition text and refuses a changed pin.
   - **State.** The tail's `aurora_veil_turns` of the side (0 to 8; nothing clears it but its end, so it outlives the snow). The residual counts it down with the other three side conditions; the two sides' same condition ending in one residual are ordered by the existing `SPEED_TIE` shuffle (decision 0007 section 6).
   - **Event (new public value, the blanket OK of 2026-10-03).** `DUOFORGE_SIDE_AURORA_VEIL` = 4, the amount of `SIDE_START` and `SIDE_END`.
   - **View.** Decision 0018: the side's `aurora_veil_turns`, public, both viewers, supported bit 3; the rows are derived from the protocol lines (`tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py`) and compared after every step (`duoforge.state.pool_g20`).
   - **Tables.** The special id AURORA_VEIL (27, after G15's EXPANDING_FORCE and G19's GLAIVE_RUSH) is new, so UNMODELED moves to 28: the pool tables, their hash and the POOL fingerprints change (CLOSURE and TEAM_C do not). The merger of a parallel step that also adds a handler id renumbers through the generator.
   - **Evidence.** Six recorded battles under the POOL kind (genders stated; seeds picked by predicates): `g20_aurora_veil_a` (cast on turn 2, hits of both categories and a spread move, the screen outlives the snow, its end line), `_clay` (Light Clay: eight turns, a critical hit that is not reduced), `_fail` (no snow: Drizzle replaced the snow; Snowscape; success; a second cast fails), `_screens` (Reflect, Light Screen and Aurora Veil on one side: one reduction per hit, a critical hit under the screens), `_pair_a` and `_pair_b` (both sides cast on one turn, the two end lines in both orders of the shuffle).
