   - **Mutation checks.** Fifteen edits (`g36_mut.py`), each killed by the conformance, the tiebreak stops, `duoforge.state.pool_g36`, the pool setup or the observation test, except one that cannot matter: the damage floor of 1 (every pool Pokemon has 16 or more maximum HP).
   - **Campaigns.** Two differential campaigns under the machine lock (seeds 36001 and 36002, 1200 battles; a team of Arbok, Umbreon, Mawile, Houndoom, Qwilfish and Sneasler with Toxic and Poison Fang against a team of attackers and each other): 1200 PASS, 0 DIVERGENCE, 0 UNSUPPORTED, with 2521 badly poisoned Pokemon, 8201 residual tox damages (stages up to 7), 843 failed Toxics, 3406 Toxics refused by the immunities and 236 Toxics that missed.
5y. **The ability that a Mega Evolution ends (a conformance fix, found by the first G29 mirror campaigns; no public value, no version bump).** `Pokemon.setAbility` runs the old ability's `End` event before it sets the new one (`sim/pokemon.ts:1923`, `singleEvent('End', oldAbility, ...)`), and the Mega Evolution's `formeChange` calls it (`:1487`). Of the 19 abilities with an `onEnd` (`data/abilities.ts`; the Champions mod has none) three are modelled: Unburden (`:5243-5245`, removes its volatile; done since step G24), **Flash Fire** (`:1351-1353`, removes the `flashfire` volatile: a Gardevoir whose Trace copied Flash Fire has it after a Fire move hit it, and the Mega Evolution ends it with `-end|X|ability: Flash Fire|[silent]`; `dfi_run_mega` kept it, so the position still showed Flash Fire) and Unnerve (`:5265-5267`, clears the `unnerved` flag of its ability state, which the engine does not keep: the foes' berries follow the ability that the holder has now). `dfi_run_mega` now clears the Flash Fire flag with Unburden's. The Trace copy (`dfi_trace`) ends nothing: the ability that it replaces is Trace itself, which has no `onEnd`. Nothing else that the engine keeps on a position belongs to an ability. Evidence: the corpus case `fz_9900100_705_min` (Gardevoir copies Ceruledge's Flash Fire, Bitter Blade hits it, it Mega Evolves), which diverged before the fix (observation, step 2) and passes after, and a mutation check (the Flash Fire clear removed: the corpus case diverges again).
5z. **G35, ten data rows, Rain Dish and Friend Guard** (the next batch of E's audit of the rev 4 blockers, `tail-rev4-proposal.md` section 6, after G30, G32, G33 and G34: rows that need no state, by blocked teams; Friend Guard alone is on 1056 of the Reg M-B teams of the 2026-10-03 survey). Twelve rows: the moves Thunder Punch, X-Scissor, Lumina Crash, Overdrive, Scorching Sands, Leaf Blade, Boomburst, Sludge Wave, Volt Tackle and Discharge, and the abilities Rain Dish and Friend Guard. No new public value, no new state, no view change, no new handler id.
   - **Deferred: Gigaton Hammer.** It was in the plan (a `cantusetwice` move: the pin refuses the same move twice in a row, and an Encore'd second use prints only a `-hint` line, which the converter and the Python reader already skip). It is not in the step because its only learner, Tinkaton, has Mold Breaker, Own Tempo and Pickpocket as legal abilities and none of them is marked, so no setup can use it, no battle can be recorded and an engine rule for it would be untested. `duoforge.state.pool_g35` fails the day one of the three is marked: Gigaton Hammer is then the next row (the rule: the second flags byte has the free bit 128, the request disables the slot whose `last_move` is the move).
   - **The pin.** Moves (`data/moves.ts`, the Champions mod overrides none of them): the data rows are the generic columns (power, accuracy, flags, the secondary kinds: Lumina Crash's 100 percent Special Defense -2, Scorching Sands' 30 percent burn with `thawsTarget`, Volt Tackle's recoil of a third, 10 percent paralysis for Thunder Punch and Volt Tackle, 30 percent for Discharge, 10 percent poison for Sludge Wave, critical ratio 2 for Leaf Blade, the target classes `allAdjacentFoes` (Overdrive) and `allAdjacent` (Boomburst, Sludge Wave, Discharge)); the flags that other rules read were added by G34 (`punch`: Thunder Punch for Iron Fist; `slicing`: X-Scissor and Leaf Blade for Sharpness; `sound`: Overdrive and Boomburst for Soundproof and Throat Chop). Abilities (`data/abilities.ts`): Friend Guard `:1533-1544`, `onAnyModifyDamage`, breakable: when the target is not the holder and is the holder's ally, `chainModify(0.75)`; Rain Dish `:3759-3770`, `onWeather`: in `raindance` (and `primordialsea`, which the format lacks) `this.heal(target.baseMaxhp / 16)`. The generator checks both texts (`G35_ABILITY_FACTS`, whole, the Champions mod overriding neither) and both abilities are `ENGINE_ROWS` (handler column NONE: the turn code reads them by id); `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` mirrors the list. The POOL table hash and the fingerprints change as for every pool step; no handler id moves (UNMODELED stays 47).
   - **Friend Guard in the ModifyDamage chain** (`turn.c`, `dfi_get_damage`). The handler is `onAny`: every active Pokemon that has not fainted runs it for every hit, so the weakening applies when the target's partner stands and holds the ability (the holder itself is not weakened; the attacker's own partner is a holder too when the attacker hits its ally). It is one more modifier, 3072/4096 (`chainModify(0.75)`), after Solid Rock and Multiscale. Like those it has no order of its own: the pin sorts the handlers by their holders' speed, then by sub-order, and this build does not compute that order but requires every order of the modifiers of one hit to chain to one value (`dfi_mods_commute`, G34). One hit now has up to six modifiers (Life Orb or Expert Belt, a resist berry, a screen, Glaive Rush's volatile, Solid Rock or Multiscale, Friend Guard), so `DFI_MODIFY_DAMAGE_MAX` is 6 and the subsets that one hit can have are 143: **85 commute and 58 do not (G34 alone: 53 of 71, 18)**. A hit with a combination that does not commute is E_UNSUPPORTED, as before; `duoforge.state.pool_g35` compares `dfi_mods_commute` with a brute-force walk of all 143 subsets and plays the refusal itself (an Expert Belt on the attacker of `g35_friend_guard`, with Multiscale and Friend Guard on its target, step 1). The bad sets with Friend Guard but without Expert Belt or Glaive Rush all have four modifiers.
   - **Rain Dish** (`turn.c`, `dfi_rain_dish`). In the weather upkeep under rain, after the upkeep line and in eachEvent's order, every active Pokemon that has not fainted and holds Rain Dish heals `floor(max HP / 16)`, at least 1, as `-heal ... [from] ability: Rain Dish` (no `[of]`): nothing is printed at full HP, and Heal Block refuses it (the common heal path). Two holders at one Speed share the shuffle of the Weather event, which draws whether or not either is damaged (the pin sorts every active Pokemon); the converter keeps the draw only in rain with two holders and drops it in any other weather (a holder has the handler under every weather and it does nothing there) and with one holder. Under Sandstorm a holder's extra handler is ignored by the Sandstorm tie rule.
   - **Converter** (`tools/reference/trace_to_c.py`). `friendguard` 3072 joins `modify_damage_values`; a ModifyDamage tie with Friend Guard twice (a holder on each side, or two on one side: the handler applies only to the holder's allies other than itself, so at most one of them applies to a hit) is dropped as one value in every order; `raindish` is an each-event handler of its holder alone (`EACH_HANDLERS`). Unit tests: `test_friend_guard_ties_step_g35`, `test_rain_dish_weather_ties_step_g35`.
   - **Evidence.** Nine recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g35_*`, seeds by predicate): `blades_punches` (`0x3502`: X-Scissor, Leaf Blade with a critical hit and Thunder Punch of Gallade, whose Sharpness boosts the slicing moves and not the punch, Thunder Punch with Iron Fist and Scorching Sands of Infernape; Steel resists the blades), `sands_thaw` (`0x351a`: Gallade's Ice Punch freezes Milotic and Torkoal's Scorching Sands, a Ground move, thaws it), `lumina_crash` (`0x3500`: -2 Special Defense answered by Competitive, Speed Boost), `toxtricity_sound` (`0x3500`: Overdrive at the foes and Boomburst at everyone, Soundproof of a foe and of the ally immune), `toxtricity_spread` (`0x3500`: Sludge Wave and Discharge hit the ally; Steel is immune to the poison hit, Ground to Discharge), `volt_tackle` (`0x3500`: recoil of a third of the damage, none against an immune target), `rain_dish` (`0x3500`: two Blastoise of equal Speed on the two sides heal in the rain that Pelipper set, the tie is drawn at each upkeep, the heals stop when the rain ends), `friend_guard` (`0x3502`: Maushold's partner Dragonite takes 0.75 with Multiscale and a Life Orb attacker in the chain, Maushold itself takes none), `friend_guard_faint` (`0x3500`: the holder is knocked out and a later hit in the same turn on its partner is not weakened). **Mutation checks.** 12 edits of `turn.c`, each built and run against the pool conformance, tiebreak, terrain, family, corpus and `pool_g35` tests: Friend Guard never applied, 0.5, protecting its holder, held by the attacker's partner, a fainted holder still weakening, missing from the commutation list, the commutation test off; Rain Dish in no weather, an eighth, no ability cause, no tie draw. **All 12 are red** (the fainted holder survived the first run, which asked for `friend_guard_faint`; the commutation-test-off edit is caught by `pool_g35` only).
   - **Campaign.** Three differential runs against the pin with two new teams (`L`: Maushold with Friend Guard beside Dragonite with Multiscale, an Expert Belt and a Life Orb among the attackers, Rain Dish and Life Dew, Volt Tackle and Discharge, the blades; `M`: Toxtricity with the sound and spread moves, a second Rain Dish holder, Scorching Sands, Recover and Life Dew at full HP and below, Iron Fist), paired with each other and with the registry teams A and B: seeds 3501 (300 battles), 3501 again after the converter fix and 3502 (400). **The first run found 11 ORACLE_GAPs of one signature** (`modifydamage-tie friendguard`: the two holders of the mirror pairing tied in one hit; fixed in the converter above, not the engine); **after the fix, 700 battles: PASS 700, 0 divergences; in all three runs 0 refusals** (a refusal is the UNSUPPORTED bucket); the domain check agreed in 1183 of 1183 and 1529 of 1529 requests of the two later runs. The teams include a full-HP heal (Life Dew, Recover) for the path that G32's loop got wrong (#180).
   - **Refusal rate of the chain** (asked for by the coordinator: above about 0.5 percent of battles the exact-order analysis of G34's text would belong in this step). Measured: 0 refusals in the 1000 battles of the three runs, whose teams were built to meet the refusal (Friend Guard, Multiscale and an Expert Belt on one side), about 21,000 steps, so below 0.3 percent of battles with 95 percent confidence even for them; estimated for the surveyed teams (`g35_fgrate` over the 43,258 parsed teams of the survey, 1510 with Friend Guard: the partner's ability and berry, a screen in the team, a Life Orb or Expert Belt on the attacker by the survey's item shares, a super effective hit one time in four): about 0.002 percent of battles, because the bad sets with Friend Guard need an Expert Belt (0.4 percent of the surveyed Pokemon) or a Multiscale or Solid Rock partner (30 of 7550 candidates) together with a berry, a screen or a Life Orb. The exact-order analysis is therefore not part of G35.
   - **Remaining refusals.** The 58 combinations of the chain (listed by `pool_g35`'s brute force), and what G34 left: two Eject Button holders at one Speed in one move, an ally under Heal Block that Life Dew would heal. Refusals met in the campaign: none.
5aa. **G27, Cursed Body and Disable** (the first users of the tail's `disable_slot` and `disable_turns`, rev 2 and 3 as they are; decision 0018 tier 3, Disable 0.29 percent of the decisions). Disable keeps a handler id of its own (`DISABLE`: its `onTryHit` and a condition whose state the tail holds), the turn code implements it, and Cursed Body is an engine row of the generator (it runs by id from the damage code). Both are marked, with the view bit 21.
   - **The pin.** Disable (`data/moves.ts:3648-3716`), Normal, Status, PP 20, accuracy 100, flags protect, reflectable, mirror, bypasssub; the Champions mod changes only the condition's `onBeforeMove` (`data/mods/champions/moves.ts:228-238`: a move with the `cantusetwice` flag is not stopped; no marked move has it). `onTryHit` (:3658-3662) fails the move (`-fail` and `[still]`) for a target without a last move or whose last move is Struggle; it is the move's own handler, run in `spreadMoveHit`, so after the accuracy (the draw comes first: recorded in `g27_disable_b`). The condition (:3663-3711): `duration: 5`; `onStart` takes one turn off (4) when the target has not taken its turn (`queue.willMove`) or when the effect is a move's own effect in progress (Cursed Body: the attacker is the active Pokemon), refuses (the move fails) a target without a last move or whose last move has no PP left, prints `-start|X|Disable|MOVE` (with `[from] ability: Cursed Body` and `[of] holder` for the ability) and stores the move id; `addVolatile` refuses a second one (no `onRestart`) and a fainted Pokemon; `onResidualOrder: 17` with `onEnd` printing `-end|X|Disable` (the duration handler is in the sorted Residual list between Encore's order 16 and Heal Block's 20); `onBeforeMovePriority: 7` (after the flinch's 8, before Throat Chop's and Heal Block's 6) stops the barred move with `cant|X|Disable|MOVE` and no PP used; `onDisableMove` bars the one slot of that move. Cursed Body (`data/abilities.ts:784-797`): `onDamagingHit`, no order (after Rough Skin's 1 and Rocky Helmet's 2), nothing when the attacker is already disabled (checked before the roll, so no draw), nothing for Struggle, a max move or a future move; otherwise `randomChance(3, 10)` (one draw per hit holder) and on a success `source.addVolatile('disable', holder)` for the attacker's last move, which is the move that just hit.
   - **State.** The tail's per-position `disable_slot` (move slot + 1, 1 to 4) and `disable_turns` (1 to 5), zero together, cleared with the occupant (a switch-out or a faint ends it with no line): no layout change. The request leaves the barred slot out; with no move left (the barred move was the only usable one: a Choice lock or an Encore on it) the slot gets Struggle, as for any disabled move. Encore keeps its own rules (it can start on a barred move; the request then offers Struggle).
   - **Rules of the turn.** The Disable move: Protect first, then the accuracy, then in the status loop its onTryHit failure (no last move, or Struggle) and `dfi_disable_start` (a start that refuses fails the move with `-fail`). Cursed Body: `dfi_cursed_body` in the DamagingHit loop of each standing damaged target, after Thermal Exchange's place and before the attacker's Poison Touch; a holder that the hit knocked out still makes its attacker roll (its onDamagingHit runs before the faint is processed: `-start|...|Disable|MOVE|[from] ability: Cursed Body` shows before the faint line, recorded in `g27_cursed_body_a`), and a fainted attacker still rolls (the roll comes first). Two holders hit by one spread move roll in the order of the move's target list, with no tie draw: DamagingHit is one of the events that `runEvent` sorts with `compareLeftToRightOrder` (`sim/battle.ts:789-790`: order, then priority, then the target's index; `:421-426`), not with `speedSort`, and the damaged targets enter as one array (`sim/battle-actions.ts:1109-1121`); Cursed Body has no order, so the second holder follows the first, and it finds the attacker already disabled when the first roll succeeded (no roll, no draw). Recorded with two holders of different Speed (the slower in slot a) and with two of equal Speed (`g27_cursed_body_spread_*`). The residual: Disable (order 17) and Heal Block (order 20) are entries of the one sorted handler list of #154 (no separate pass), at their own orders, and callbacks exactly when they end now; the engine's sort draws a group of equal handlers when two of its entries are callbacks, so the draw order follows the reference by construction (the side conditions' tie, order 26, comes after) and the tie draw is the reference's own (the converter keeps it as it is; before, it was rewritten to the order of the positions, which a displacing selection sort does not guarantee). The insertion-order refusal (#154) stays: the turn's counters (Protect, stall, flinch, a charge, Helping Hand, Follow Me) are added by this turn's action and so come after a Disable or Heal Block that ends now, which the exact test knows; every other pair of volatiles of one Pokemon is of unknown order.
   - **New public values (the blanket OK of 2026-10-03, names and numbers agreed with the coordinator).** `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_DISABLE` = 4 (the detail of `VOLATILE_START` and `VOLATILE_END`; START carries the barred move in `id`, and for Cursed Body the cause `ABILITY` with the ability in `id2` and the holder in `other`) and `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_DISABLE` = 19 (`CANT`, the barred move in `id`), in their own commit; and the internal draw site `CURSED_BODY` = 17 (random(10) < 3, named by `ps_trace.js` from the effect and the event).
   - **View.** Decision 0018: `disable_slot` of the position, public (`-start|X|Disable|MOVE`: the slot of MOVE on the open sheet), supported bit 21, both viewers; the turns are never shown.
   - **Tables.** The special id DISABLE (47, the last of the handlers, after G34's 46 that main had when this step was merged) is new, so UNMODELED moves to 48 (the generator checks the move's `onTryHit` and both condition texts, the pinned and the Champions one), and Cursed Body becomes an engine row (the handler column of its row is NONE); `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` runs the pinned callbacks of both (the failures, the durations, the lines, the order, the bar and the roll).
   - **Evidence.** Eleven recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g27_*`; three of them `g27_cursed_body_spread_a`, `_b` and `_tie`: two Cursed Body holders on one side hit by one Heat Wave, both rolling in slot order, the first success leaving the second without a roll, and equal Speed with no `SPEED_TIE` draw): `g27_cursed_body_a` (Cursed Body rolls, a start with `[from] ability: Cursed Body`, a holder that the hit knocks out still disabling its attacker), `g27_disable_b` (the move on a target that has moved, five turns, and on one that has not, four; the failure with no last move and after the accuracy draw; a second Disable fails; the `cant` line of a queued barred move), `g27_disable_lock` (Disable with Encore and a Choice lock: the request offers Struggle), `g27_disable_switch` (an already disabled attacker hits a holder: no roll; a switch-out clears the bar without a line), `g27_disable_pair_a` and `_b` (two Disables ending in one residual at equal Speed, both orders of the shuffle), `g27_disable_pp` (Disable after the last PP of the target's move, and on a target whose last move is Struggle: `-fail`) and `g27_disable_heal_block` (Disable and Heal Block ending in one residual: Disable first). 247 pool battles in all after the merge with main (167 before it), 94 view rows checked for both viewers (`duoforge.state.pool_g27`: the tail and the view field after every step), 1369 tiebreak stops (916 before it).
   - **Mutation checks.** Twenty-six edits (`g27_mut.py`), each killed by the conformance battles, the tiebreak stops or `duoforge.state.pool_g27`, except three: the redundant `last == Struggle` test of Disable's own failure (the start refuses a last move of 5 anyway: an equivalent edit), Disable's order 17 moved to 21 (Heal Block's pass is fixed after Disable's, and no other handler lies between: an equivalent edit), and a fainted attacker that Cursed Body disables (no recorded battle has a Rocky Helmet or Rough Skin kill with a successful roll, so the refusal of a fainted occupant by `dfi_disable_start` is read from the pin, `addVolatile`, not recorded).
   - **Campaigns.** Three differential campaigns under the machine lock, teams of Gengar, Banette and Froslass (Cursed Body, Disable) against a spread-move team and each other (seeds 27001, 27002 and 27003, 800 battles): 800 PASS, 0 DIVERGENCE, 0 UNSUPPORTED. The first version of the step refused a spread hit on two Cursed Body holders (`E_UNSUPPORTED`, 47 of 400 battles, 11.75 percent) because it assumed the holders' order was a Speed order; the pin's order is the target's index (`compareLeftToRightOrder`), the refusal is gone, and the same two seeds of 400 battles now all pass. The 800 passing battles hold 1628 Disable starts (883 by Cursed Body), 730 `-end` lines, 119 `cant` lines of a barred move, 564 failed Disable moves and 3165 Cursed Body draws. After the residual change (the sorted list) two more campaigns (seeds 27101 and 27102, 1000 battles): 1000 PASS, 0 DIVERGENCE, 0 UNSUPPORTED, with 2186 Disable starts (1198 by Cursed Body), 970 ends, 166 `cant` lines, 741 failed Disable moves and 3975 Cursed Body draws. Mutation checks after it: 26 edits, all killed but the two equivalent or unrecorded ones above (Disable's order 17 moved to 21 or 15 is now killed too).
5ab. **G25, Electric Terrain and Misty Terrain** (the second and third terrain after Psychic Terrain, on the path of step G15: Electric Terrain 1.10 percent of the replay decisions, decision 0018 tier 1; Misty Terrain tier 4). The step marks the two terrain moves, the entry setter Electric Surge, the two seeds, and the two moves that read the terrain: Rising Voltage and Terrain Pulse. Tables: `ELECTRIC_TERRAIN`, `MISTY_TERRAIN`, `RISING_VOLTAGE` and `TERRAIN_PULSE` are new named handler ids 28 to 31 (`G25_HANDLERS` of the generator; UNMODELED moves to 32), the terrain family column has the codes 3 (Electric) and 4 (Misty), which are the engine's `DFI_TERRAIN_*`, Electric Surge is a terrain setter and Electric Seed and Misty Seed are `ENGINE_ROWS` items, so the POOL table hash and the fingerprints change as for every pool step. Misty Surge is not in the pool. **Public values** (additive, approved by the owner (2026-10-03)): `DUOFORGE_FIELD_ELECTRIC_TERRAIN` = 4 and `DUOFORGE_FIELD_MISTY_TERRAIN` = 5, the detail of a FIELD_START and FIELD_END event (own commit); the terrain values 3 and 4 and the view bits 5 and 32 are the ones of decision 0018 and are now produced; the line `-activate|target|move: X Terrain` that a status refused by a terrain shows is the generic ACTIVATE event with the terrain move as its cause (no new value).
   - **The pin.** `data/moves.ts`: Electric Terrain 4497-4555, Misty Terrain 12151-12208 (the Champions mod overrides neither; both are `terrain:` moves with a condition of 5 turns and `durationCallback` 8 for Terrain Extender, which is not marked), Rising Voltage 15137-15156, Terrain Pulse 19265-19301; `data/items.ts` Electric Seed 1799-1821 and Misty Seed 4200-4222, which are Grassy Seed (2595-2614) for their terrain and Defense or Special Defense (the generator checks the text, `SEED_PAIRS`); `data/abilities.ts` Electric Surge 1179-1187 (`onStart` calls `setTerrain`, the same pattern as Psychic Surge). The generator reads the whole callbacks of both terrains, of Rising Voltage and of Terrain Pulse as `G25_FACTS` (whitespace aside) and `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` calls the pinned handlers (`checkTerrains`): the modifiers, the refusals and which of them print a line, the durations, the seeds, Terrain Pulse's types and power, Rising Voltage's power.
   - **setTerrain** (`sim/field.ts:130-157`, called from moveHit, `sim/battle-actions.ts:1252-1255`): the same terrain is not restarted and the move fails (`-fail`, as for the weather moves), a different one replaces it for 5 turns without a line for the old one, `-fieldstart|move: X Terrain` with `[from] ability: Electric Surge` and `[of]` for the ability, and then `TerrainChange` runs the seeds (`dfi_terrain_change`). The ability and the seeds run as for Psychic Surge and Psychic Seed.
   - **Damage.** Electric Terrain: x5325/4096 for a grounded user's Electric move (onBasePowerPriority 6, the slot of Grassy Terrain and Psychic Terrain: one terrain at a time), Misty Terrain: x0.5 (2048/4096) for a Dragon move at a grounded target (the defender's grounding), one chained modifier with the others. Rising Voltage: `basePowerCallback`, 140 for a grounded target in Electric Terrain whoever the user is (the move's own 5325 follows); Terrain Pulse: `onModifyMove` doubles the power in any terrain for a grounded user and `onModifyType` gives the terrain's type (Electric, Grass, Fairy, Psychic) for a grounded user. The user's and the target's grounding are the Flying type alone, as for Expanding Force (Levitate, Air Balloon, Ingrain, Gravity, Magnet Rise, Smack Down and Roost are UNMODELED rows; `test_pool_rows.py`).
   - **Status.** `Pokemon.setStatus` (`sim/pokemon.ts:1684`): the already-has check and the type immunities first, then the SetStatus event. `Battle.resolvePriority` gives a speed only to the effects of a Pokemon (`sim/battle.ts:1001-1003`) and `comparePriority` sorts by speed before the sub-order (:404-411), so the terrain's `onSetStatus` (the field's handler, speed 0) runs after Thermal Exchange and Flower Veil, which refuse first and end the event; the engine checks the terrain after them and before the sleep counter is drawn. Electric Terrain refuses sleep for a grounded Pokemon, Misty Terrain every status, both by returning false; the `-activate` line shows for a move's own status (Electric: `effect.effectType === 'Move' && !effect.secondaries`; Misty: `effect.status`, which a move whose status is a secondary does not have) and for neither a secondary, an ability (Poison Touch) nor an item. Misty Terrain's `onTryAddVolatile` stops confusion on a grounded Pokemon (the line for a move without secondaries; the engine adds only a secondary's confusion, so no line). Yawn, Rest, the Electric Terrain Yawn refusal and every other source are UNMODELED rows.
   - **The lines.** Misty Terrain's `onFieldEnd` prints `-fieldend|Misty Terrain` with no "move: " (`data/moves.ts` 12198-12200 at the pin; the others have it); the converter maps it, and only it, to the Misty end event. The converter's state table has the terrain values 3 and 4.
   - **What stays unmarked** (each reads the terrain by a callback of its own and so is an UNMODELED row; `test_pool_terrain.c`): Surge Surfer, Mimicry, Seed Sower, Grass Pelt, Terrain Extender, Ice Spinner, Misty Explosion. **Steel Roller** is a handler since step G34 and ends Grassy and Psychic Terrain there; this step extends its terrain end to Electric and Misty Terrain (`dfi_clear_terrain`: the end line is `-fieldend|move: Electric Terrain` and, without the "move: ", `-fieldend|Misty Terrain`), and gives Terrain Pulse the type of each of the four terrains. The Grassy Terrain and Psychic Terrain moves stay UNMODELED rows too (the terrain comes from the setters; not part of this step).
   - **A defect fixed on the way: the -ate boost of a Weather Ball.** The `-ate` abilities leave the moves of the `noModifyType` list of their `onModifyType` alone (`judgment`, `multiattack`, `naturalgift`, `revelationdance`, `technoblast`, `terrainpulse`, `weatherball`; `data/abilities.ts`), and only a type that the ability itself changed gets `typeChangerBoosted` and the 4915/4096. The engine skipped the type change for Weather Ball but still gave the boost when the weather's type equalled the ability's (a Refrigerate holder's Weather Ball in snow: Ice). `dfi_ate_excluded` (Weather Ball and Terrain Pulse) now guards both places. The regression battle is `g25_weather_ball_refrigerate` (Aurorus with Refrigerate, Abomasnow's Snow Warning); the mutation that takes the guard out turns `conformance_pool_data` red.
   - **A second defect, found by the campaign: Lightning Rod and the type of the move.** `getMoveTargets` runs its `RedirectTarget` event (`sim/pokemon.ts:829-831`) after `ModifyType` (`sim/battle-actions.ts:430-438`), so the redirection of Lightning Rod looks at the type the move has when it is used: a Terrain Pulse in Electric Terrain is Electric and is drawn to a Lightning Rod holder (Special Attack +1, `-activate|holder|ability: Lightning Rod`, the move line retargeted). The engine read the table type (Normal). `dfi_move_type_now` is the one function of the type (Struggle, the -ate abilities, Weather Ball, Terrain Pulse) and both the redirection and the hit steps call it; the four cases of the campaign are in the corpus (`fz_2501_{26,74,90,228}_prefix`: a mismatched target, the missing Special Attack stage, a later fainted holder and the invariant that the wrong target broke).
   - **Evidence**: eight recorded POOL battles (genders stated, seeds by predicate; the eighth, `g25_steel_roller_terrains` (seed `0x2500`), came with the re-sync onto G34: Electric Surge's terrain ended by Aggron's Steel Roller, Misty Terrain set by Sylveon and ended by the next one with its line without "move: ", the third one failing without a terrain): `g25_electric_surge_voltage` (Electric Surge sets the terrain at the start with its ability line, Rising Voltage from a grounded user at a grounded and at an airborne target, the move's fail when the terrain is up, Hypnosis refused to a grounded target with the Electric Terrain line and a flying one put to sleep, the end after five turns), `g25_electric_seed` (a seed holder that switches in while the terrain is up), `g25_misty_terrain` (Misty Terrain with its seed, Dragon Claw at a grounded target before the terrain and halved after it, a Dragon move at a Fairy, Will-O-Wisp refused, the end line), `g25_terrain_pulse_psychic_misty` (Psychic, then Fairy), `g25_terrain_pulse_electric` (Electric, super effective), `g25_misty_hurricane` (Hurricane's confusion at grounded targets in Misty Terrain: the secondary's roll succeeds and no confusion starts), `g25_weather_ball_refrigerate` (the defect). 163 pool battles and 880 stops (156 and 838 before: six per new battle, counted by hand from `tiebreak_ref.h`: 42 added, none removed), 82 marked moves (78 before), `duoforge.state.pool_terrain` (the terrain after every step against the field lines, both players, with the extension zero; the rows are derived from the traces by `test_trace_to_c.py`), the ability-family test (Terrain Pulse and Weather Ball are left alone by every `-ate` ability, three terrain setters), the generator tests (`TerrainFacts`, the seeds, the handler order). Mutation of the engine rules, 21 edits (the terrain's boost, Misty's factor and its grounded test, the doublings of Rising Voltage and Terrain Pulse, Terrain Pulse's type, the sleep refusal for the airborne and the paralysis instead of sleep, the all-status refusal and its -activate line, the failing of the same terrain, the length of a terrain, the seed's stat, the field detail, the end line, Electric Surge's terrain, the -ate guard of Weather Ball, Lightning Rod's type, the confusion refusal): 20 turn at least one of `conformance_pool_data`, `tiebreak_pool_data`, `pool_terrain` and the ability-family test red (the Lightning Rod one the corpus test), one stays green (below). A differential campaign (the two teams of the step against each other and teams A and B, 240 battles, seed 2501, POOL data kind, under the machine lock) gave 225 PASS and 15 DIVERGENCE of four signatures with the one cause above; fixed, the same campaign gave 240 of 240 PASS. Not exercised by a recorded battle, and so by no test but the reasoning above: Terrain Pulse's type for an airborne user (no learner is a Flying type; the mutation that drops the grounded test of its type stays green).
5ac. **G26, Perish Song** (1.58 percent of the replay decisions, decision 0018 tier 1; the first user of the tail's `perish` counter, rev 2 as it is: no new field, no new state). Perish Song keeps a handler id of its own (`PERISH_SONG`, 48 after Disable; UNMODELED moves to 49): its `onHitField` and its condition are code, not columns; the generator checks both texts whitespace aside. One new public value, the owner's assignment of 2026-10-03: `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_PERISH = 5` (id 4 is Disable's), the library version 0.35.0.
   - **The pin** (the Champions mod has no entry of the move: `data/mods/champions/moves.ts` is hashed by the generator and has none; its learnsets give it to seven formes, of which Politoed, Primarina and Wigglytuff have a marked ability). `data/moves.ts:13233-13277`: Normal, Status, accuracy true, PP 5, flags `sound distance bypasssub metronome` (no `protect`), target `all`. `onHitField` (:13242-13260) goes over `getAllActive()` (`sim/battle.ts:1365`: every active Pokemon that has not fainted, side and slot order) and for each: a `false` Invulnerability event misses it (`-miss`, counts as a result; no marked move makes a Pokemon semi-invulnerable), a TryHit that returns null stops it silently (a result too), otherwise, if it has no `perishsong` volatile, `addVolatile` (duration 4) and `-start|X|perish3|[silent]`; a Pokemon that has it is left alone. If nothing was a result the move returns false: `-fail|user` with `[still]` (`moveHit`, `sim/battle-actions.ts:1302-1308`, reached through `tryMoveHit` for a target `all`, :505-507, :838-840, :1039-1042, :1267-1272); otherwise, if at least one Pokemon got the volatile, `-fieldactivate|move: Perish Song`. The TryHit handlers of the battle: Good as Gold (`data/abilities.ts:1630-1641`, a status move of another Pokemon: `-immune|X|[from] ability: Good as Gold`, the user's own is not stopped), Flash Fire and Lightning Rod (read the type, Normal here), Armor Tail and Psychic Terrain (read a priority above 0: refused below); Protect and Wide Guard let a move without the protect flag through (`checkMoveBypassesProtect`, `sim/battle.ts:1300-1309`; `data/moves.ts:13982-14000`), Substitute is bypassed; Soundproof (`:4436-4452`, a sound move of another Pokemon: `-immune|holder|[from] ability: Soundproof`, the volatile is not given, and it counts as a result) was unmarked when this step was written and is marked by step G32, so the cast handles it (`g26_perish_soundproof`). Perish Body (`:3227-3239`, adds the same volatile on contact) is unmarked too.
   - **The condition** (`:13261-13272`): `duration: 4`, `onResidualOrder: 24`, `onEnd` shows `-start|X|perish0` and calls `target.faint()` (hp 0, queued: `sim/pokemon.ts:1581-1593`), `onResidual` shows `-start|X|perishN` with N the duration after the decrement. `Battle.fieldEvent` (`sim/battle.ts:484-575`) sorts every handler of the residual once (order, priority, Speed, sub-order; a tie is a shuffle, so a draw), and for each: a handler with a duration counts down, and at 0 its `onEnd` runs and the loop `continue`s with **no faintMessages**; any other handler (a callback, or a duration that has not run out) is followed by `faintMessages()`, which processes every faint queued so far and checks the win. So the cast turn's residual shows perish3, the next perish2, perish1, and the fourth shows perish0 for each holder in Speed order, then the faints: after the first later handler that does not end (a non-final count of a slower holder, a side condition or terrain that does not run out, White Herb, the stall counter or a charge that does not end), else after the `upkeep` line (`sim/battle.ts:2811-2818`, :2835); a battle that ends there has no `upkeep`. `faintMessages` (:2535-2604) and `checkWin` (:2606-2617): when every side is out the winner is `faintData.target.side`, the side of the last Pokemon processed, which here is the slowest holder. Switching out or fainting clears the volatile with no line (`clearVolatile`).
   - **State and rules in the engine.** The tail's `perish` is the duration: 4 from the cast to the residual of that turn, then 3, 2, 1, 0 (the invariants' bound 4 was already there). `dfi_run_perish_song` (`src/combat/turn.c`) is the cast above; the residual list gets a Perish entry (order 24, sub-order 2, a callback: its tie draws like Encore's), the first loop runs the sorted prefix (the callbacks, with Heal Block's and Throat Chop's duration entries between Encore's 16 and Perish's 24 skipped: they run in their block), and the faint timing of `fieldEvent` is implemented where it shows: `dfi_residual_faints` after a side condition, Trick Room or terrain that does not run out, after White Herb, and before the stall counters when one of them does not end. A Heal Block that ends in the same residual as a Perish count is refused (E_UNSUPPORTED): its end line would come before the counts in the reference and after them in the engine's block; the cheap and the exact order tests of `residual_order.h` count the Perish handler as a volatile of its Pokemon. A move with priority above 0 (Prankster) is refused for the same reason as Armor Tail and Psychic Terrain. Two things that the first campaign found: a holder whose faint is queued (hp 0, `faint()` called) still has its volatiles until `faintMessages` clears them, so its stall counter that does not end is a faint point, while a holder whose faint was processed has none (the engine clears the slot when it processes the faint) and its White Herb handler is skipped (`sim/battle.ts:525-554`: the state is not at its place any more); and the two sides' Tailwind (or Reflect, Light Screen, Aurora Veil: order 26, one sub-order each) tie in the sort, a shuffle that the conversion drops when the two only count down. The same holds for the handlers that have no order (the stall counter, a charge, Protect's and Helping Hand's duration): they come last, in the Speed order of their holders (`comparePriority` puts no order after every order), and a faint point at one that does not end can end the battle with the handlers after it unrun, so the ones before it have ended and the ones after it keep their counters; the engine now runs them in Speed order (it ran them in position order before; three campaign cases showed it). The tie between one Pokemon's Protect volatile and its stall counter (equal keys: one holder, one Speed, one sub-order; every Protect turn has one, a draw `random(start, start + 2)` of the reference's sort) shows in one case only: the battle ends at the faint point that follows the stall counter, and a Pokemon that still stands has kept Protect's volatile when the stall counter ran first and lost it when Protect's own handler did (24 of 24 and 17 of 18 reference survivors in the campaigns that found it). The engine draws it then (`dfi_residual_pair_draws`, `src/combat/turn.c`: the battle ended in the residual and a standing Pokemon has exactly those two handlers; the draw is spent unseen when the end was earlier, at a Perish count or a side condition) and the conversion keeps exactly that draw (`no_order_end_tie`, `tools/reference/trace_to_c.py`, next to `side_end_tie` and `heal_block_end_tie`): the group is the reference's list order, which the state does not hold, so the entry states the outcome (0 the stall counter ran first, 1 Protect's volatile did), and a uniform shuffle makes the engine's own draw the same distribution. A tie of more than the pair (an equal-Speed neighbour, Helping Hand's volatile too) or a survivor with another handler stays `E_UNSUPPORTED` when the battle ends there; a Pokemon that fainted has lost its volatiles, so it never matters. The tie of the sides' screens now shows when a faint is queued and exactly one side's counter ends in this residual (the faints come after the one that does not end, before or after the other's `-sideend`), and when the battle ends at the first side's faint point (the other side's remaining turns show it): both are `E_UNSUPPORTED`. A faint that no damage line announced shows the Pokemon at 0 in the foe's knowledge (`dfi_events_fold_knowledge`): the screen shows it gone, and the knowledge invariant needs it.
   - **Events (the new public value).** `DUOFORGE_EVENT_VOLATILE_START` with `detail` `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_PERISH` (5) and `amount` the count N (3, 2, 1, 0): one per holder and residual; no END (the holder faints, or leaves with no line). `-fieldactivate|move: Perish Song` is `DUOFORGE_EVENT_ACTIVATE` with cause MOVE, `id2` the move and no position (documented in `duoforge.h`); `-fail` and `-immune` are the existing events. The converter maps exactly these lines, refuses every other `-fieldactivate`, and a `faint` line sets the foe's seen HP to 0.
   - **View.** Decision 0018: the position's `perish`, public, both viewers, the supported bit 4 (`PERISH`): the last count announced; the state keeps 4 from the cast to the first count, and the view shows 0 then (a request in the middle of the turn, a pivot's). The rows of `duoforge.state.pool_g26` are derived from the protocol lines by `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py` and compared after every step of every battle, for both viewers, with the tail.
   - **Evidence.** Five recorded POOL battles (`g26_perish_survivor_a` and `_b` are the last two: a Pokemon that the second cast did not infect wins with Protect, and the tape's tie entry says whether it keeps it); the first three (genders stated; the first two seeds are the defaults): `g26_perish_song` (Politoed's cast with Protect users and a Good as Gold ally, a Kingambit that switches out and loses the volatile, a second cast that infects the newcomer only and is stopped by Good as Gold again, the four counts, and the faints before the `upkeep` line because a Protect user's stall counter does not end), `g26_perish_recast` (Incineroar's Parting Shot in the middle of the cast turn, a second cast by the other Politoed at the same Speed whose residual handlers tie, the newcomer infected, a third cast that fails, and the faints of two holders before a slower holder's count and then the third, the interleaved case of `fieldEvent`) and `g26_perish_end` (two cycles; the second ends the battle: four holders faint together, with a Tailwind that does not end: the faints come at that handler and the battle ends there, no `upkeep`, and the win goes to the side of the last to faint; its first cycle ends with a Protect of the turn before whose stall counter runs out in that residual, which is a handler that ends and so does not bring the faints forward: they follow the `upkeep` line). `duoforge.reference.conformance_pool_data` replays all of them with the reference's draws, `duoforge.reference.tiebreak_pool_data` with the reference's tiebreak (two of its stops are an ended battle), `pool_families.js checkG26` pins the facts of the move, the condition, `onHitField` against stubs, Good as Gold and Soundproof.
   - **Mutation checks.** Thirty-three edits (twenty-six, then seven of the pair draw below), each killed by `conformance_pool_data`, `tiebreak_pool_data`, `duoforge.state.pool_g26`, `duoforge.combat.residual_order` or `duoforge.state.pool_tail`: the cast not stored, with duration 3, resetting the holders; Good as Gold not immune; a recast that does not fail; `-fieldactivate` always printed; the count not decremented or off by one; the faint at 0 not queued, or processed at once; no faint processing after a count that goes on, after a side condition that does not run out, or at a stall counter that does not run out; faint processing at a stall counter that does run out; the Heal Block refusal removed; the Perish handler not a volatile of the order analysis; the view without the field, or showing the duration; the faint not in the knowledge; the two refusals of the side conditions removed (`duoforge.state.pool_g26` sets the Tailwinds by hand); a queued holder that loses its stall counter, the White Herb of a processed holder as a faint point, the no-order handlers in position order instead of Speed order, and the refusal for a standing survivor's own two handlers removed or applied to a fainted one (five entries of the differential corpus, `fz_9261_2_prefix`, `fz_9261_3_prefix`, `fz_9272_126_prefix`, `fz_9273_116_prefix` and `fz_9274_284_prefix`, which the campaigns found, and `duoforge.state.pool_g26`). The pair draw (`dfi_residual_pair_draws`): its outcome ignored or inverted, applied when the battle ended elsewhere, the end not recorded, Protect's volatile cleared before the stall counter's faint point (all five killed by `duoforge.state.pool_g26`, which appends the tape's tie entry to a recorded step and reads the survivor's Protect flag, for both outcomes and for an earlier end at a Tailwind); the pair taken to include a third handler and the refusal of an equal-Speed neighbour removed survive: no recorded battle and no state that the test can make reaches them (a Helping Hand flag set before the turn is no valid state: E_INVARIANT), only the campaigns do. The converter's `no_order_end_tie` has its unit test and four of five edits killed (`conv_any_group_size` is equivalent: a pair is two entries). The one earlier edit that survives is the refusal for two standing Pokemon of equal Speed at that faint point. Not exercised by a recorded battle, and said so: a cast that skips a fainted-not-yet-replaced slot, the user's own Good as Gold (equivalent: it is not stopped), the White Herb and the Trick Room and terrain points of the faint timing, and the Prankster refusal.
5ad. **G33, multi-hit moves and Mirror Armor** (no new public value, no new event kind, no view change, no state). Marked: **Dual Wingbeat** and **Twin Beam** (`multihit: 2`, `data/moves.ts`), **Triple Axel** (`multihit: 3`, `multiaccuracy: true`, `basePowerCallback` of 20 x the hit number) and the ability **Mirror Armor** (`data/abilities.ts:2657-2679`). Tables: two handlers `MULTI_HIT_2` (52) and `TRIPLE_AXEL` (53) after the four of G25, UNMODELED 54 (`G33_HANDLERS`); the hit counts live in the handler ids, not in the static `multihit` columns (decision 0020 item 5 has no engine reader). The hit loop is the Champions format's own (`data/mods/champions/scripts.ts:427-549`, the base game's `sim/battle-actions.ts:857-979`): every hit rolls its critical hit and its damage, runs the DamagingHit handlers (Rocky Helmet and Rough Skin cost the user HP after every hit) and an Update; the faint messages come once after the loop; a multiaccuracy move checks its accuracy again before each later hit (with its own arithmetic, found by the first campaign: the loop scales the accuracy by the two stages one after the other in floating point with no floor, `accuracy /= boostTable[-boost]` (`scripts.ts:481-510`), where the first hit's `hitStepAccuracy` floors; the accuracy 90 at the stage -1 is 67.5 and the draw 67 hits. The engine has no floating point outside the tiebreak, so it compares `v x D < N` for the exact rational, and `checkG33` proves that this equals the pin's binary64 result for every pair of stages: 169 of 169), a miss ends the loop silently; the loop also ends when the target is at 0 HP, the user has fallen asleep or fainted; recoil, Life Orb and the AfterMoveSecondary handlers run once after the loop. A spread move with more than one hit is refused (`E_UNSUPPORTED`). **Population Bomb stays unmarked** (`multihit: 10`, multiaccuracy): its only user, Maushold, has no marked ability, so no battle that uses it can be recorded; the generator pins its data (`checkG33`) and nothing else. **Mirror Armor**: each lowered stat of the holder that another Pokemon caused is not applied; if that Pokemon is standing, it gets the same drop (a `-ability|holder|Mirror Armor` event, then the boost with Mirror Armor as the effect: no second bounce); a drop that Clear Body or a veil blocks is not bounced. Parting Shot keeps its switch when its drop was bounced (`moves.ts:13174-13180`). **The converter** maps the derived line `-hitcount|P|N` as an assertion that N equals the -damage lines of that move on P and drops it (rule `hitcount-mismatch`); it asserts only when the line is present (the Champions loop prints none for Parental Bond when only the first hit landed, `scripts.ts:547-548`). Whoever builds Substitute must count the hits into a Substitute too (`-activate|P|move: Substitute|[damage]`, `-end|P|Substitute`): they are not -damage lines, yet N counts them. `tools/reference/ps_trace.js` classifies the multiaccuracy `randomChance` called from `hitStepMoveHitLoop` as an ACCURACY draw (it was UNKNOWN; harness version unchanged). **Evidence**: seven recorded POOL battles (`g33_dual_wingbeat`, `g33_dual_wingbeat_items`, `g33_triple_axel`, `g33_triple_axel_accuracy`, `g33_triple_axel_stages`, `g33_twin_beam`, `g33_mirror_armor`: hit counts 1, 2 and 3, Triple Axel misses, a Protected target, a Focus Sash, a Life Orb that knocks the user out, a Dark type that is immune, Intimidate and Icy Wind bounced, Defiant and Competitive answering, Parting Shot bounced twice with the user still switching; and a Sitrus Berry eaten between the two hits, an Emergency Exit holder that leaves after the second); `tools/datagen/pool_families.js` calls the pinned callbacks (`checkG33`). Mirror Armor with a fainted source and with a Grass-type holder are pinned in `checkG33` only, not recorded. **Known gaps**: the later-hit arithmetic is proved for the accuracy 90 only, so a multiaccuracy move with another accuracy is refused (`combat/multiaccuracy.h`; a new such move extends the proof in `checkG33` first); and a target's Emergency Exit after a multi-hit move reads the HP before the whole move, where the pin reads `hurtThisTurn` plus `move.totalDamage` (the two differ only when the target was hurt earlier in the turn and its first hit crosses half; no Emergency Exit holder is a legal target of a multi-hit move of the pool in that way today, so the case is unreachable).

5ae. **G38, Imprison** (184 of the Reg M-C and 694 of the Reg M-B teams; the volatile that the tail's `imprison` flag of rev 2 was reserved for). One handler id, `IMPRISON` (47; UNMODELED moves to 48); the move is marked; the two public values `DUOFORGE_VOLATILE_IMPRISON` = 8 (VOLATILE_START only) and `DUOFORGE_CAUSE_IMPRISON` = 21 (CANT, the stopped move in `id`) and the producing of view bit 2 (`IMPRISON`, supported bit 12) were approved by the owner.
   - **The pin** (the Champions mod has no entry of the move): `data/moves.ts:9489-9523`. `onStart` (`:9501`) prints `-start|user|move: Imprison` (the target is the user, `target: "self"`); the volatile has no duration and ends with the occupant (`sim/pokemon.ts:1508` `clearVolatile`, on a switch-out or a faint, with no line). `onFoeDisableMove` (`:9504-9510`) runs for every active Pokemon in `endTurn` (`sim/battle.ts:1685-1695`) and calls `disableMove(slot.id, true)` for every move of the imprisoner (Struggle aside): a **hidden** disable (`sim/pokemon.ts:1625-1636`; one that is already `true` stays). `onFoeBeforeMove` (`:9511-9519`, priority 4) stops a move that the imprisoner knows (`hasMove`, its current move slots; not Struggle) with `cant|X|move: Imprison|Move` and no PP used: it matters for a move that was queued before the Imprison was used this turn, and for an Encored one. A second Imprison of a Pokemon that has it fails (`-fail`, the move line with `[still]`; there is no `onTry`).
   - **The request.** A hidden-disabled move is shown as enabled in the request of the last active Pokemon of a side and as disabled for the others (`sim/pokemon.ts:1025-1027`, `isLastActive` `:1192`), but the choice is validated against `getMoves()`, where hidden counts as disabled (`sim/side.ts:718-737`): the choice of an imprisoned move is rejected. With every move so disabled `getMoves()` is empty and the choice is Struggle (`sim/pokemon.ts:1119`, `sim/side.ts:699-706`), which Imprison does not forbid. The engine's request (`dfi_slot_candidates`, `request.c`) does not offer the move: it is exactly the set that Showdown accepts, which the domain check of the random mode compares. **Information:** Showdown hides that the imprisoner knows the move (the request shows it enabled for the last active Pokemon and the rejection comes only on the choice); the engine's mask removes it, so the request of the foe tells which moves the imprisoner knows. Under open team sheets (Reg M-C now) the moves are public and nothing leaks; under closed sheets (the planned M14) it would, and a request for a player there has to show the moves as enabled and reject the choice instead (the `hidden` rows of the harness mark them).
   - **Interactions.** *Encore* (`:4754-4775`): its `onDisableMove` disables every other slot, Imprison the encored one: no move is left, the choice is Struggle, and Encore's `onOverrideAction` does not replace Struggle (recorded: `g38_imprison_encore`); an Encored move that is queued when the Imprison comes is stopped by `cant`. *Choice lock* (`data/conditions.ts:324-363`): the lock disables the other slots and Imprison the locked one: Struggle, which the lock's `onBeforeMove` lets pass (`id !== 'struggle'`), and the lock stays (`g38_imprison_choice`). *Two imprisoners* hide the union of their moves; their `onFoeBeforeMove` handlers have the same priority, so a tie of their speeds is a draw in the reference whose order shows in nothing (either handler stops the move with the same line): the converter drops it (`event:BeforeMove` of Imprison handlers only), and the `DisableMove` tie too (every handler only sets `disabled`).
   - **Engine** (`turn.c`, `request.c`): `dfi_run_imprison` (the flag and the start event, or the failure), `dfi_imprison_forbids` and its check in `dfi_before_move` after Throat Chop and Heal Block (6) and before the confusion (3), the request mask. The flag is the tail's (rev 2: no new state), cleared with the occupant (`dfi_tail_clear_occupant`). Under the POOL kinds only. The view: bit 2 of the position's volatiles from the flag, public for both viewers, supported bit 12.
   - **Converter and harness.** `-start|X|move: Imprison` is VOLATILE_START (detail 8), `cant|X|move: Imprison|Move` is CANT (cause 21, id the move); an `-end` of it is refused. The reference state's `imprison` volatile is compared through the lines, the requests and the view bit. `ps_trace.js` records, only while a move is hidden-disabled, `hidden` per active slot next to `enabled` (a state without the key is what every earlier trace has: no harness version change), and `trace_to_c.py` takes the hidden moves out of the request's move mask and makes Struggle of a request with none left.
   - **Evidence.** Eight recorded battles under the POOL kind (genders stated; `g38_*`): `g38_imprison_mask` (the masks of two foes), `_struggle` (only move imprisoned), `_cant` (a queued move stopped, the stopped move the imprisoner's last), `_encore`, `_choice`, `_end` (a second Imprison fails; the volatile ends with a switch-out), `_pair` (two imprisoners), `_struggle_b` (a foe whose only move is imprisoned, in the last active slot: the request still shows it, any `move N` is Struggle, `sim/side.ts:699-706` before the disabled check; the converter's `convert_choice` reads the `hidden` rows for it). `duoforge.state.pool_g38` (the tail's flag after every step, the events, both viewers' extension; rows derived from the protocol lines by `test_trace_to_c.py`). Mutation checks (seven edits of `turn.c`, `request.c` and `observation.c`): the request not masking, the mask on the imprisoner's own side, no `cant`, a second Imprison not failing, no view bit and the last move slot not forbidden are each killed by the conformance or `pool_g38`.
5af. **G29, the item-transfer moves Trick, Switcheroo, Thief and Covet** (Trick blocks 8 of the 227 survey teams). Four handler ids (`TRICK` 56, `SWITCHEROO` 57, `THIEF` 58, `COVET` 59; UNMODELED moves to 60), so the pool tables, their hash (850cc218...) and the POOL fingerprints (KP 68962f87..., KPD 01d22794...) change once; the four rows are marked; view bit 11 stays exact (the item that a move gave is `item_now` too).
   - **The pin** (the Champions mod changes none of the four): `data/moves.ts:19865-19911` Trick, `:18644-18690` Switcheroo, `:19302-19330` Thief, `:3099-3123` Covet; `sim/pokemon.ts:1851-1889` takeItem and setItem; `sim/battle.ts:571-650` singleEvent (the handler's arguments are the relayed item, the event's target, its source); `data/abilities.ts:4622-4635` Sticky Hold, `:5240-5242` Unburden; `data/items.ts:27` the Mega Stones' onTakeItem. The generator checks the whole text of each callback.
   - **Trick and Switcheroo.** `onTryImmunity` (the hit step before accuracy) fails a target with Sticky Hold: `-immune|target`, no reason. `onHit` takes the target's item (the user is the taker), then the user's own; a refusal of either (a Mega Stone held by its own species), two empty hands, or the second TakeItem check of an item with the other Pokemon as its receiver (a Mega Stone is refused for a receiver of the species it belongs to; the handler's second argument is the receiver) restores both items and fails: `-fail|user` with `[still]`. Then `-activate|user|move: Trick|[of] target` (Switcheroo prints Trick too), and for the target, then for the user, `setItem` and `-item|X|Item|[from] move: M`, or, when the other one had nothing, the silent `-enditem|X|Item|[silent]|[from] move: M` of the item that left. Unburden's volatile is set whenever the TakeItem handlers ran for an Unburden holder that holds an item, also when the move then fails.
   - **Thief and Covet.** `onAfterHit` (at Knock Off's point, also for a user that has fainted since: the Champions mod does not ask): nothing if the user holds an item; the target's `takeItem(user)` (a live Sticky Hold target shows `-activate|X|ability: Sticky Hold` and keeps the item; a Mega Stone is not taken from its own species); the second TakeItem check with the user as receiver; `setItem(user)`, which refuses a user that has fainted (the item goes back, the target's TakeItem handlers have run). Thief shows the silent `-enditem|target|Item|[silent]|[from] move: Thief|[of] user` and `-item|user|Item|[from] move: Thief|[of] target`; Covet only the second line, with its own move.
   - **State and rules.** The held item is the tail's `item_now` (G16): an item id + 1 for an item that a move gave (the consumed flag of the receiver is cleared), 255 for none. A Choice lock ends with the item (a Choice item's onStart removes it; a Scarf that arrives locks at the Pokemon's next move as always). Under the POOL kinds a member without a sheet item may have used an item that a move gave it (the tail's `item_now` names it), and the Unburden volatile may stand with an item held (a swap that gave an item back, or one that failed after the TakeItem handlers ran): `tests/test_pool_g29.c` (`check_kind_rules`) runs one state under TEAM_C_DEV, where both stay refused, and under POOL_DEV. **A transfer that would make the receiver use the item on the spot** (setItem's onStart: a White Herb with a lowered stat, a terrain seed under its terrain) **is `E_UNSUPPORTED`**, decided before anything changes; every other onStart of the marked items does nothing here.
   - **Events (owner's OK pending for the new value).** `DUOFORGE_EVENT_ITEM_START` = 42: position the receiver, `id2` the item + 1, cause MOVE with `id` the move, `other` the Pokemon it came from when the line says `[of]`. The silent `-enditem` of Trick, Switcheroo and Thief is ITEM_END with the cause ITEM_TAKEN (no other silent line is an event); `-activate|X|move: Trick` is the ACTIVATE that every move's `-activate` already is. The converter reads exactly these shapes and refuses any other `-item` or `[from] move:` `-enditem` line. The old `item_used` of the player view is defined on its own commit (owner's OK pending): the sheet item is gone and nothing is held.
   - **Evidence.** Four recorded battles under the POOL kind: `g29_trick_scarf` (Leftovers and a Choice Scarf change hands, the Scarf locks its new holder), `g29_empty_hands` (Switcheroo and Trick to and from empty hands, Unburden), `g29_thief_covet` (Covet takes a Sitrus Berry; Thief does nothing while the user holds an item; Sticky Hold blocks it twice; a Mega Stone is not taken; a Rocky Helmet is taken after it hurts the thief), `g29_trick_fails` (a stone refused for its receiver's species, a Mega Stone refused for its holder, Sticky Hold and Good as Gold `-immune`, two empty hands). `duoforge.state.pool_g29` reads the tail and both viewers' extension after every step against rows that `tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py` derives from the protocol lines.
5ag. **Mega batch 2: Swampertite, Metagrossite, Lucarionite Z with Aura Guard, and Froslassite** (the outlier Mega block of the owner's wind-down; per 100 teams in `usage.json`: Metagross-Mega 6.135, Lucario-Mega-Z 4.990, Froslass-Mega 4.573, Swampert-Mega 3.625). Four stones on the Mega-by-stone model of 5p, and one engine rule. No public value, no new state, no view change, no version bump (the library version stays what main has).
   - **The pin.** The stones carry no Champions rule (`data/mods/champions/items.ts`: `inherit: true, isNonstandard: null`). Each Mega forme has one ability (`data/pokedex.ts`): Swampert-Mega Swift Swim (G22), Metagross-Mega Tough Claws (step 11), Froslass-Mega Snow Warning (5d), Lucario-Mega-Z **Aura Guard** (`data/abilities.ts:310-319`: `onSourceModifyDamage`, `if (move.flags['contact']) return this.chainModify(0.5)`, `flags: {breakable: 1}`, tagged `isNonstandard: "Future"`, which the pinned validator accepts for the Mega forme; the Champions mod has no entry). Base abilities: Swampert Torrent, Metagross Clear Body (G30), Lucario Inner Focus (G22), Froslass **Cursed Body** (step G27; the other, Snow Cloak, is not marked and is not needed).
   - **Aura Guard** (`turn.c`, `dfi_get_damage`): one more modifier of the ModifyDamage chain, 2048/4096, for a hit with the contact flag on a holder of the ability. It is the target's one ability, so a hit has at most one of Solid Rock, Multiscale and Aura Guard (`DFI_MODIFY_DAMAGE_MAX` stays 6) and the commutation test of G34 covers it: of the 191 subsets that one hit can have, **113 commute and 78 do not** (G35: 143, 85, 58); the new refusals are the Expert Belt with Glaive Rush or with a Friend Guard partner at an Aura Guard holder, like Multiscale's. `breakable` matters only with Mold Breaker, which is not marked. Aura Guard is an engine row of the generator (`ENGINE_ROWS`, fact text checked by `MEGA2_ABILITY_FACTS`; handler NONE, 153 unmodelled abilities), and the converter knows its modifier (`modify_damage_values`, the exclusive group of the target's ability).
   - **Marks.** The items Swampertite, Metagrossite, Lucarionite Z and Froslassite (handler NONE already) and the ability Aura Guard. The support of a pair is that of its Mega ability (5p). Lucarionite (the other Lucario stone, Adaptability) stays unmarked: nobody asked for it.
   - **Tests.** `duoforge.state.pool_mega2` (new, white-box): the marks, the Mega of each pair and its ability, Aura Guard as the only ability of one forme, `dfi_mods_commute` against a brute force over the 191 subsets, and the engine's refusal itself (the Close Combat of `mb2_aura_guard_chain` is played with Life Orb, Reflect and Aura Guard, and with a Friend Guard on the Reflect user; with an Expert Belt in place of the Life Orb it is E_UNSUPPORTED). `test_trace_to_c.py` (`test_aura_guard_ties_mega_batch_2`) and `test_gen_closure.py` pin the converter and the generator side.

The directory is also part of the cloud watchdog's rotation. The POOL table hash and the fingerprints change as for every pool step; UNMODELED moves 252 to 236, abilities 154 to 141 (`tools/datagen/pool_families.js` agrees).

   - **Abilities.** Hyper Cutter: a foe's drop of Attack is refused with `-fail ... unboost atk [from] ability: Hyper Cutter` (not for a move's secondary, not for the holder's own drops). Queenly Majesty shares Armor Tail's TryMove block (priority moves at the holder or its allies fail). Sturdy: a hit at full HP leaves 1 HP, before Focus Sash (also confusion damage); its OHKO immunity is inert because no OHKO move is marked (`pool_g39` pins it). Damp is inert: every move that its callbacks stop (Explosion, Mind Blown, Misty Explosion, Self-Destruct, Aftermath) is unmarked (guard test). Snow Cloak and Sand Veil: the evasion rider x3277/4096 for the target in snow or in sand, in G34's accuracy chain per target (after Compound Eyes, Wide Lens last); Sand Veil also makes its holder immune to the sand's damage. Static: a contact hit paralyses the attacker 3 times in 10 (site 19, `randomChance(3, 10)`, called next to Flame Body). Justified: a Dark move that hits raises Attack by one stage (after Stamina and Thermal Exchange). Limber: no paralysis from any source (`-immune` for a move's primary status, silent for a secondary; a Flower Veil holder stays E_UNSUPPORTED), and Trace refuses to copy it onto a paralysed holder. Solar Power: Special Attack x1.5 in sun and upkeep damage of 1/8 of the maximum HP at the end of each turn of sun (`[of]` is the holder), in the weather's each-order. Regenerator: switching out heals floor(max HP / 3) before the switch, as the Champions `[silent]` heal; the engine emits it as a HEAL event with the ability cause (the opponent's HP display follows the event) and the converter keeps that one `[silent]` line. **The Python tracker needed no fold**: `python.replay`, `python.live` and the equivalence tests pass with the `g39_regenerator` battle, and nothing in `python/` changed.
   - **Moves.** Charm and Fake Tears: `dfi_boost` with the primary effect at the target. Super Fang: `dfi_get_damage` returns max(1, floor(current HP / 2)) with no draws (the accuracy of 90 is the normal check). Sacred Sword: Darkest Lariat's handler.

A step that marks one of them reads these lines for Taunt and Yawn. Misty Terrain and Electric Terrain are not in this build.

No draw site is new.

5aj. **G37, the entry hazards** (Stealth Rock, Spikes, Toxic Spikes and Sticky Web, and Toxic Debris; the tail's four layer fields of each side, rev 2, and `hazard_order`, rev 4; the owner's OK of 2026-10-03 for the four new public `SIDE` values). The four moves with the target class foeSide become modelled rows, Toxic Debris an engine row, and the switch-in handlers of the hazards run in the sorted order of the reference's `SwitchIn` event.
   - **The pin** (the Champions mod overrides none of them; `tools/datagen/gen_closure.py` checks the whole texts, `G37_FACTS` and `G37_ABILITY_FACTS`). Stealth Rock `data/moves.ts:17814-17839`: `onSwitchIn` (unless Heavy-Duty Boots, which are Past in the format) damages `maxhp * 2 ** typeMod / 8`, the Rock effectiveness against the Pokemon's types clamped to [-6, 6], through `damage()` (floor, at least 1); grounded or not. Spikes `:17500-17525`: one to three layers (`onSideRestart` stops at 3), a grounded Pokemon takes `[0, 3, 4, 6][layers] * maxhp / 24`. Toxic Spikes `:19749-19788`: one or two layers; a grounded Pokemon: Poison type absorbs them (`-sideend|side|move: Toxic Spikes|[of] POKEMON`, the condition is gone), a Steel type takes nothing, else `trySetStatus('psn' or 'tox' with two layers, foe.active[0])` (no `-fail` or `-immune` line: the condition is the source effect, not a move; Flower Veil's `onAllySetStatus` blocks it silently). Sticky Web `:17935-17956`: a grounded Pokemon: `-activate|X|move: Sticky Web`, then `boost({spe: -1}, X, foe.active[0], the move)`: Clear Body, Flower Veil, Contrary, Defiant and Competitive react as to a move's own drop. Toxic Debris `data/abilities.ts:5104-5117` (`onDamagingHit`): a Physical hit adds a layer of Toxic Spikes to the attacker's side (the foe's side when an ally hit the holder) while it has fewer than two, `-activate|holder|ability: Toxic Debris` first, also for a holder the hit knocked out. The moves are `foeSide` status moves, accuracy true: `getMoveTargets` takes every foe position (`sim/pokemon.ts:796-807`), `tryMoveHit` runs `TryHitSide` (no Protect, no accuracy), and `getRandomTarget` names a random foe in the move line that the converter leaves out (the draw is `RANDOM_TARGET execute:foeSide`, dropped like a spread move's). A fifth layer, a second Stealth Rock or Sticky Web and a third Toxic Spikes fail (`-fail`, `[still]`).
   - **Switch-in order (the new part of the turn code).** `fieldEvent('SwitchIn')` collects the hazards of the entering Pokemon's side as handlers with the Pokemon as holder (`sim/battle.ts:502`), sorted by priority, speed (the shuffled order of `runSwitch`), sub-order (a side condition 4, before an ability's 7) and then `effectOrder`, the order in which the conditions were created (`:994-1000`, "they should activate in the order they were created"). The tail's `hazard_order` (rev 4: one byte per side, two bits per slot, a new kind appended, a further layer keeping its slot, an ended kind removed with the later ones moving down, a kind put up again last) is that order; `dfi_hazards_enter` runs the handlers in it, skipping one that an earlier handler removed (`:540-543`). A Pokemon with a hazard handler is a bearer for the draw of the speed tie of `runSwitch` (the harness counts the side's handlers, `tools/reference/ps_trace.js`, and the converter keeps the draw when two bearers tie, as for entry abilities). `fieldEvent` runs `faintMessages` after each handler (`:569-570`): a Pokemon that a hazard knocks out has its faint shown at once and no handler after it, neither another hazard nor its ability (found by the first campaign: Stealth Rock, then no Sticky Web line). After the runSwitch action the first Pokemon of the batch runs the `EmergencyExit` event when a hazard took it to half (`sim/battle.ts:2871-2874`).
   - **State and view.** The four layer fields and `hazard_order` (invariant: the first n slots are the kinds that are up, once each, the others 0). The view fills `stealth_rock`, `spikes`, `toxic_spikes` and `sticky_web` of both sides (supported bits 13, 30, 8 and 33); the order is not public.
   - **New public values (the owner's OK).** `DUOFORGE_SIDE_STEALTH_ROCK` 5, `_SPIKES` 6, `_TOXIC_SPIKES` 7 and `_STICKY_WEB` 8, the `amount` of `SIDE_START` and `SIDE_END` (a further layer of Spikes or Toxic Spikes is another `SIDE_START` of the same amount; a Toxic Spikes absorption is a `SIDE_END` with the absorbing Pokemon in `other`). No new cause: the damage is `CAUSE_MOVE` with the move's id (`[from] Stealth Rock` and `[from] Spikes` are the condition's own names, as Spiky Shield's), and Sticky Web's activation is an `ACTIVATE` with the same cause. The foeSide target class (15) and the four view fields existed.
   - **Tables.** No handler id: the move rows carry the side condition (the column holds the `SIDE` value) and the target class 15, so UNMODELED moves go down by four and the unmodelled abilities by one (Toxic Debris).
   - **Not in this step.** Hazard removal (Rapid Spin, Defog, Mortal Spin, Court Change, Tidy Up) and Ceaseless Edge and Stone Axe (a hazard after a hit, `onAfterHit`) stay unmarked, a later batch. Heavy-Duty Boots are Past, so no holder skips a hazard; Air Balloon (`isGrounded`), Magic Guard (it blocks `damage()` from a condition), Magic Bounce (`reflectable`) and Pressure (`mustpressure`) are unmarked, so no accepted battle has them: a step that marks one of them reads these lines. Corrosion (Glimmora's other ability) is unmarked too.
   - **Evidence.** Nine recorded battles under the POOL kind (`g37_*`, seeds by hand): `hazards_a` (the build-up of all four, the failures, flyers, a Steel type with the web and Defiant, a Water type with the rock, the spikes, the bad poison and Competitive), `toxic_spikes` (a flyer, bad poison, a Steel type, a Poison type absorbing and the layers put up again), `sticky_web` (Clear Body, White Herb, Competitive and a flyer), `toxic_debris` (a special hit, two Physical hits and a third that adds nothing; the attacker's switch-ins meet the layers and the rock), `order_a` (Spikes before Stealth Rock, Toxic Spikes before Sticky Web: the lines in that order), `order_b` (Toxic Spikes first, absorbed, put up again: they come last) and `hazard_faint` (a Pokemon that falls to the spikes: the faint at once, nothing after it), `exit` (Emergency Exit of a Pokemon that a hazard brings under half, after the switch) and `flower_veil` (Toxic Spikes and Sticky Web against Flower Veil: the foe is the source). The rows of `duoforge.state.pool_g37` (the layers, the creation order and the view of both viewers, after every step) are derived from the reference's own state (the harness records the side's hazards in creation order) and from the protocol lines (`tools/reference/test_trace_to_c.py`).
   - **Converter.** `-sidestart` and `-sideend` of the four hazards, the damage causes, the move line of a foeSide move without a target, the `RANDOM_TARGET execute:foeSide` draw, and the boundary of a switch request: the harness records the actions still queued (`queue_len`), and a switch request with an empty queue is a REPLACEMENT also when the step has no `upkeep` (a replacement that a hazard knocked out at once; the old rule called it a PIVOT).
   - **Mutation checks.** 26 one-change mutants of `src/combat/turn.c`, the manifest and the observation (the rock fraction and type, the spikes amounts and the flyer, absorption, Steel and the bad poison, the web stages, flyer and line, the order and its slots, Toxic Debris category, side and layers, a fourth layer, the failed hazard, the entry bearer, Emergency Exit after the switch, the move line target, the Flower Veil source, the faint after each handler, the supported bits, the view): every one is caught by `conformance_pool_data`, `tiebreak_pool_data` and `pool_g37`, the marking and view mutants also by `pool_tables` and `observation_ext`. The first run left three survivors; two led to the battles `exit` and `flower_veil`, the third (a Steel type poisoned) is the same behaviour, because the status code refuses Poison and Steel anyway.
   - **Campaigns.** `diff_driver.py random` under POOL with hazard teams: 1200 battles (500 and 700), 0 divergences and 0 unsupported; a kept set of 300 passes 300/300 and covers 2448 `SIDE_START`, 1062 Stealth Rock and 1277 Spikes damages, 285 Toxic Spikes statuses, 396 Sticky Web activations, 259 absorptions, 215 Toxic Debris and 169 hazard faints. The two divergences that the campaigns found (a Pokemon that a hazard faints has no later handler; a replacement that dies at once is a REPLACEMENT) are fixed and are in `hazard_faint` and the converter.
5ak. **G41, Shadow Tag and Gengarite** (the Mega ability of Gengar; no new public value, no new event, no state, no view change). Marked: the ability **Shadow Tag** (`data/abilities.ts:4156-4173`; the Champions mod does not override it) and the item **Gengarite** (its base ability Cursed Body is G27's). **No handler**: the ability is an engine row read by id. The turn code has one predicate, `dfi_switch_trapped` (`src/combat/turn.c`), and the request builder (`src/state/request.c`, `dfi_slot_candidates`) asks it only at a **TURN** boundary: a standing Pokemon with a standing foe that has Shadow Tag, that is neither a holder itself nor a Ghost type (by its current types: `typechart.ts:204`, `trapped: 3`; Soak's Water makes it trappable again), is offered no switch, in either slot. In doubles every foe is adjacent (`isAdjacent`), so the foe's both actives count. Replacements, pivots (a move's selfSwitch, Emergency Exit, Eject Button) and forced switches ignore it (the reference's `canSwitch` for those is the reserve test alone); the trap is recomputed from the board at the next TURN boundary, so a holder that has fainted or left by then traps nobody (`nextTurn` clears `trapped` and runs `TrapPokemon` again, `sim/battle.ts:1726-1730`).
   - **Why the exclusion leaks nothing** (decision 0007, the information rule of 0018 section 1: a fact is public when the game shows it or it follows from the open team sheets and the lines). At the pin the first active gets `trapped: true` and the last active only `maybeTrapped`; a switch of the latter is refused with `[Unavailable choice]` and an updated request under a new rqid (`sim/side.ts:527-534`, `984-1000`, `sim/pokemon.ts:1112-1134`), which is how the game hides whether a foe might have a trapping ability. Under Open Team Sheets that doubt does not exist: the Mega forme and its single ability Shadow Tag follow from the Gengarite on the sheet and the `-mega` line, a Trace copy is a `-ability|X|Shadow Tag|Trace|[from] ability: Trace|[of] Y` line (the view's `ability_now`, decision 0018 section 6.1), a type is the forme's or Soak's `-start|X|typechange|Water`. The trap state is therefore a pure function of public information, and a domain without the switch tells the player nothing it cannot derive. The engine offers no `maybeTrapped` state and no refusal code; the differential harness maps the pin's last-active asymmetry instead (below).
   - **The cases, derived or refused.** (1) Ability suppression: Neutralizing Gas is not a row of the pool, Gastro Acid is unmarked, Mold Breaker does not touch it; (2) Skill Swap, Role Play, Entrainment, Simple Beam, Worry Seed, Wandering Spirit and Mummy are unmarked (Trace is the one that moves an ability, and its line is public); (3) a holder is exempt itself, and its ally is not trapped by it (`pokemon.hasAbility('shadowtag')`, only foes are asked); two holders on both sides trap each other's partner only; (4) Ghost immunity by current types, Soak included; (5) Shed Shell and Run Away (`onTrapPokemon` at priority -10, after Shadow Tag's, `data/mods/champions/abilities.ts:71-80` for the ability) are unmarked, so no battle has them; (6) a holder that faints or leaves in the turn does not trap at the next turn (the boundary is recomputed from the board), and the PIVOT and REPLACEMENT boundaries in between are free; (7) the first and the last active are treated alike, because both are derivable. Illusion is unmarked. `tests/test_pool_g41.c` names each unmarked row that would free, move, suppress or hide: marking any of them fails there until `dfi_switch_trapped` reads it. Python has no rule: the live bot still reads Showdown's request (HauptSession's flow for `[Unavailable choice]`).
   - **Harness** (`tools/reference/ps_play.js`, no recorded value): the sampler reads each Pokemon's own `trapped` flag (the request of the last active has only `maybeTrapped`), and the domain check accepts exactly one request change, the refused switch of the last active that turns its `maybeTrapped` into `trapped: true` and marks the request `update: true` (`sim/side.ts:521-534`; an `rqid` change would do too), keeping the sample; any other change, or that one without the marker, stays a dropped sample. Measured: a first campaign (seed 4143) judged 1407 requests, 98 of them changed in this way (111 requests carried `trapped: true`, 30 only `maybeTrapped`); with the marker accepted, a second campaign (seed 4144) drops 0 of 1446.
   - **Evidence**: six recorded POOL battles (`g41_shadow_tag_second`: the Gengar in the second position of its side, so a probe of the foe's first position alone fails; `g41_shadow_tag_fainted`: the foes take down the Gengar's ally and both reserves and then the Gengar, which stays in its position with no reserve, and from the next TURN boundary traps nobody; and the four that follow: `g41_shadow_tag`: the Gengar Mega Evolves at turn 1 and both foes are trapped from turn 2, with a U-turn pivot; `g41_shadow_tag_ghost`: a Gholdengo free, its partner trapped and pivoting; `g41_shadow_tag_soak`: a Dragapult trapped after Soak; `g41_shadow_tag_trace`: a Gardevoir's Trace copies Shadow Tag after a replacement, traps the Kingambit beside the Gengar and is itself exempt), `tests/test_pool_g41.c` (the domain at the turns of those battles, the pivot boundaries and the guard), `checkG41` (the pinned handlers on stubs and the one immune type).
